TL;DR: RECAP-Forcing introduces appearance-novelty memory for long video generation, storing KV caches only when content first appears to maintain long-range consistency without growth tied to video length; it unifies an initial attention sink with an optical-flow novelty bank in a training-free inference framework that improves quality over baselines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Long autoregressive video models are constrained by finite attention windows, forcing a choice between retaining recent frames or compressing older ones. This leads to potential loss of identity consistency for subjects, objects, and scenes over long horizons.

Method: Organize memory by appearance novelty: preserve the KV cache of newly appearing content at first visibility. Use an initial attention sink for the novel scene at video start; extend with an optical-flow-based novelty bank to retain newly revealed content as the video evolves. The approach requires no additional learnable parameters and operates during inference.

Result: The method reportedly yields consistent improvements in visual quality and semantic fidelity across strong baselines and outperforms existing memory methods.

Conclusion: Appearance-indexed memory makes long-range consistency an explicit property of the memory structure, with memory growth driven by newly introduced content rather than video length, unifying two mechanisms under a single principle and enabling training-free, improved long-video generation.

Abstract: Long autoregressive video generation faces a fundamental memory challenge: with a finite attention window, a model must decide which information from an ever-expanding history to retain. Existing methods organize memory temporally, preserving recent frames while compressing or discarding older ones. We instead propose RECAP-Forcing, organizing memory by appearance novelty. A long video is not merely a sequence of frames, but an evolving cast of subjects, objects, and scenes whose identities must remain consistent over time. We organize memory by retaining the KV cache associated with newly appearing content--such as entering subjects, disoccluded regions, and newly introduced scenes--at the moment it first becomes visible, prioritizing novelty over recency. Memory should scale with the amount of newly introduced content, rather than with video length. This appearance-indexed memory makes long-range consistency an explicit property of the memory structure. Our framework unifies two mechanisms under this single principle. At the beginning of a video, when all visible content is novel, an attention sink preserves the initial scene. As the video evolves, an optical-flow-based novelty bank extends the same principle by selectively retaining newly revealed content. As a training-free inference method with no additional learnable parameters, RECAP-Forcing consistently improves visual quality and semantic fidelity across multiple strong baselines and outperforms existing memory methods.

</details>


### [27] [Reason in the Words You Speak: Idiolectal Paraphrasing Off-Policy Traces for Reasoning Distillation in VideoLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26684)
*Ji Soo Lee,Jinyoung Park,Seohyun Lee,Jongha Kim,Joonmyung Choi,Jinsung Yoon,Hyunwoo J. Kim*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Echo-GRPO rewrites privileged reasoning traces into the student’s own idiolect via Dual-Reference Decoding, enabling policy-aligned supervision for reasoning distillation. VideoEcho-R1 demonstrates consistent gains across multimodal LLM backbones and five benchmarks; the approach improves both RL and supervised fine-tuning, indicating broader applicability beyond GRPO.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: On-policy GRPO limits reasoning advancement and prior off-policy traces cause gradient clipping due to mismatch with the student policy distribution. Aligning supervision to the student’s idiolect could unlock higher-order capabilities.

Method: Introduce Echo-GRPO, a framework that converts teacher traces into the student’s idiolect using Dual-Reference Decoding, preserving semantics while matching expression. Instantiated as VideoEcho-R1 for video reasoning distillation, evaluated across three multimodal backbones and five benchmarks. Demonstrates that idiolectal paraphrasing is a plug-in module improving both RL and supervised fine-tuning.

Result: Consistent improvements across three multimodal LLM backbones and five benchmarks for video reasoning distillation. Idiolectal paraphrasing improves both reinforcement learning and supervised fine-tuning frameworks, indicating policy-aligned supervision benefits beyond GRPO.

Conclusion: Policy-aligned supervision via idiolect paraphrasing is a general, plug-in approach for reasoning distillation that extends beyond GRPO to improve diverse training regimes for reasoning in LLMs.

Abstract: Recent large language models achieve strong performance on complex reasoning tasks, where reinforcement learning with Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) has emerged as a leading paradigm for optimizing models on self-generated trajectories. However, the on-policy nature of GRPO bounds the model to the reasoning skills it can already produce, restricting to learn more advanced capabilities. Prior works inject privileged reasoning traces from a stronger teacher policy to guide training, yet these traces are inherently out of distribution with respect to the student policy. We observe that this mismatch between on-policy and off-policy causes gradient clipping on semantically critical reasoning tokens, ultimately rewarding correct answers while leaving the reasoning that justifies them unlearned. Hence, we propose \textbf{Echo-GRPO}, a framework that lets the model reason in the words it speaks. Rather than imitating low-probability privileged traces from the teacher model, Echo-GRPO rewrites them into the student policy's own \textit{idiolect}, that is, its own characteristic vocabulary and expression patterns, while preserving their semantics via Dual-Reference Decoding. We instantiate this framework as \textbf{VideoEcho-R1} for video reasoning distillation, achieving consistent improvements across three multimodal LLM backbones and five benchmarks. Finally, we show that our idiolectal paraphrasing is a plug-in module that consistently improves both RL and supervised fine-tuning frameworks for reasoning distillation, demonstrating that policy-aligned supervision extends beyond GRPO.

</details>


### [28] [Domain-Specific Self-Supervised Representation Learning for Retinal Fundus Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26686)
*Bekzat Nurlanbekova,Fung Fung Ting*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Contrastive SSL (SimSiam, SimCLR) for retinal disease classification under data scarcity; shows augmentation-tuning and lightweight training enable transferable representations with limited data/computation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Medical imaging often lacks labeled data; SSL using unlabeled fundus images can learn useful representations. Domain-specific augmentation may enhance SSL performance for retinal tasks.

Method: Empirical study of SimSiam and SimCLR under resource-constrained settings with small batch sizes and retinal-specific augmentations; evaluation via linear probing and fine-tuning on downstream retinal tasks (multi-disease classification, diabetic retinopathy grading).

Result: Tailoring augmentation to retinal images significantly improves representation quality; even with constrained resources, lightweight SSL learns transferable representations and achieves competitive results, reducing dependence on large labeled datasets.

Conclusion: Domain-aware SSL is viable for retinal imaging under resource constraints; augmentation design is a key lever for performance; supports broader adoption of SSL in medical imaging with limited annotations.

Abstract: Despite the growing number of public datasets, annotated medical images remain scarce. Supervised learning methods achieve strong performance on many benchmarks, however require large amounts of labeled data, which are costly and time-consuming to obtain in the medical domain. To address this limitation, contrastive self-supervised learning (SSL) has emerged as a promising alternative for learning useful representations from unlabeled data. In this work, we investigate two SSL frameworks, SimSiam and SimCLR, for retinal disease classification from fundus images. We focus on understanding how augmentation strategies and training parameters influence representation learning under resource-constrained settings. Given limited data and computational capacity, we explore the feasibility of training SSL models with small batch sizes incorporated with retinal-specific augmentation techniques. Through a series of experiments, we assess the quality of learned representations via linear evaluation and fine-tuning across downstream tasks, including multi-disease classification and diabetic retinopathy grading. Our results show that tailoring augmentation strategies to the characteristics of retinal images plays a critical role in improving performance. Even under constrained settings, lightweight SSL frameworks can learn transferable representations that reduce dependence on large annotated datasets and achieve competitive results.

</details>


### [29] [AesCanvas: A Large-Scale Dataset and Benchmark for Aesthetic Critique and Contextual Suitability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26713)
*Xuanwei Hu,Haoyu Dong,Kejun Wu,Tianyi Liu,Jianjun Gao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: AesCanvas provides a unified benchmark for multimodal LLMs to generate critique and to judge contextual aesthetic suitability, revealing a split between critique quality and context-sensitive judgments, and showing that aesthetic specialization does not guarantee contextual performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current IAA benchmarks target intrinsic visual quality or fixed domain criteria, but fail to assess an image's suitability for a given purpose, audience, culture, or domain convention. There is a need for culturally grounded, context-aware evaluation of aesthetics.

Method: Two components: CritiqueCanvas with 519,136 instruction-response pairs from 54,300 images enabling long-form, multi-dimensional critique across photography, painting, and virtual imagery; ContextCanvas with 301 expert-reviewed use scenarios evaluating contextual aesthetic suitability in realistic use cases. A unified protocol evaluates closed-source frontier, open-weight general, and aesthetic-specific MLLMs.

Result: Findings indicate a separation between critique generation and context-sensitive judgment: lexical/semantic, reference-based metrics partially capture critique quality. Aesthetic specialists can perform well on certain critique metrics but lag behind strong general-purpose MLLMs on ContextCanvas. Aesthetic specialization does not reliably transfer to contextual suitability, and model decisions may fail to ground themselves in decisive contextual cues.

Conclusion: Culturally situated, evidence-grounded suitability is established as a distinct objective for aesthetic modeling, beyond traditional intrinsic-quality assessment.

Abstract: Recent advances in Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have extended Image Aesthetic Assessment (IAA) beyond scalar scores toward interpretable critique and guidance. Yet existing benchmarks mainly assess intrinsic visual quality or fixed domain criteria, leaving open whether an appealing image is appropriate for a specific purpose, audience, cultural setting, or domain convention. We introduce AesCanvas, a unified suite with two complementary components: CritiqueCanvas with 519,136 instruction-response pairs from 54,300 images supports long-form, multi-dimensional critique across photography, painting, and virtual imagery, whereas ContextCanvas with 301 expert-reviewed use scenarios evaluates contextual aesthetic suitability in realistic use scenarios. Under a unified protocol, we evaluate closed-source frontier, open-weight general, and aesthetic-specific MLLMs. Results reveal a clear separation between critique generation and context-sensitive judgment: reference-based lexical and semantic metrics only partially capture critique quality, while aesthetic specialists remain competitive on selected critique metrics yet substantially lag strong general-purpose MLLMs on ContextCanvas. Further analyses show that aesthetic specialization does not reliably transfer to contextual suitability and that model decisions may fail to track or ground themselves in decisive contextual visual cues. These findings establish culturally situated, evidence-grounded suitability as a distinct objective for aesthetic modeling.

</details>


### [30] [LiveVVT: High-Fidelity Video Virtual Try-On in Real Time](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26714)
*Yushe Cao,Shikun Feng,Ruxiang Duan,Liyong Wang,Dianxi Shi,Chun Yu,Junliang Xing*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: LiveVVT proposes a streaming diffusion framework for video virtual try-on that uses a sliding window for bounded bidirectional denoising, two long-term memories to ensure consistency, and a progressive distillation training regime. It achieves real-time performance with significantly reduced latency and higher throughput compared to similar-sized models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Reduce latency and compute overhead of full-clip diffusion-based video virtual try-on while preserving bidirectional priors and synthesis quality for long video sequences.

Method: Rolling streaming diffusion within a fixed-size window that jointly denoises multiple chunks with bounded look-ahead, emitting one clean chunk per iteration. Long-term consistency is maintained via a bounded temporal memory and a persistent global appearance memory (built from the target garment and a frontal try-on keyframe). A progressive distillation framework integrates bidirectional VVT learning, teacher-trajectory regression for causal few-step adaptation, and Collaborative Matching Distillation (teacher-distribution matching + rolling flow matching on real videos) to align optimization with recurrent inference.

Result: Experiments on paired and unpaired long-sequence benchmarks show superior generation quality versus similarly sized models, with 26× lower latency and 11× higher throughput, enabling high-fidelity real-time streaming VVT.

Conclusion: LiveVVT delivers real-time, high-fidelity streaming video virtual try-on by combining bounded bidirectional modeling within a sliding window, two complementary memories for long-term consistency, and a progressive distillation training strategy, achieving substantial latency reductions without sacrificing quality.

Abstract: Diffusion-based Video Virtual Try-On (VVT) achieves high visual fidelity through bidirectional spatio-temporal modeling, but complete-clip dependence incurs prohibitive latency and computational overhead in practical continuous deployment. Naively enforcing causality disrupts pretrained bidirectional priors and substantially degrades synthesis quality. We introduce LiveVVT, a rolling streaming diffusion framework that preserves bounded bidirectional modeling within causal recurrent generation. Within a fixed-size window, LiveVVT jointly denoises multiple video chunks under bounded look-ahead, preserving local bidirectional interactions while emitting one clean chunk per iteration. Beyond the window, two complementary memories sustain long-term consistency: a bounded temporal memory propagates recent dynamics and occlusion context, whereas a persistent global appearance memory, constructed once from the target garment and a frontal try-on keyframe, anchors garment details and dressed appearance throughout the stream. We further introduce a progressive distillation framework integrating bidirectional VVT learning, teacher-trajectory regression for causal few-step adaptation, and Collaborative Matching Distillation, which couples teacher-distribution matching with rolling flow matching on real videos to align optimization with recurrent inference. Experiments on paired and unpaired long-sequence benchmarks demonstrate superior generation quality over similarly sized models, with $26\times$ lower latency and $11\times$ higher throughput, enabling high-fidelity real-time streaming VVT.

</details>


### [31] [Beyond Atomic Layouts: Compositional Design Understanding with Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26716)
*Yiyang Huang,Zhaowen Wang,Simon Jenni,Jing Shi,Yitian Zhang,Yizhou Wang,Yun Fu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: CoDeLayout provides a ~20K real-world multi-layer layout VQA dataset for compositional understanding and introduces MASON, a post-training method that combines multimodal alignment and structural perception to address semantic drift and hierarchical ambiguity, achieving state-of-the-art data-efficient gains over baselines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing vision-language models handle individual, independent layout elements but struggle with compositional layouts requiring reasoning over entangled, hierarchical structures. Semantic drift between metadata and visuals and structural ambiguity impede performance.

Method: Introduce CoDeLayout dataset (~20K layouts) with compositional element pairs and design intent. Propose MASON, a post-training paradigm consisting of Multimodal Alignment (MA) to ground textual metadata to visuals, and Structural Perception (SP) to model layer-aware inter-element spatial relationships.

Result: Baseline GPT-o3 achieves 79.68% accuracy. Qwen2.5-VL 7B with MASON reaches 91.66%. MASON outperforms full-data Direct Finetune using only 30% of data and scales better with more data.

Conclusion: MASON effectively mitigates semantic drift and structural ambiguity, enabling improved compositional layout understanding. The approach demonstrates strong data efficiency and scalability for VLMs on hierarchical layouts.

Abstract: Layout understanding, or the interpretation of element organization, is essential for document analysis, user interface (UI) creation, and graphic design. While recent vision-language models (VLMs) excel at interpreting atomic layouts composed of independent elements, they struggle with compositional layouts that require reasoning over visually entangled elements within hierarchical multi-layer structures. In this paper, we introduce a new task, compositional layout understanding, and present CoDeLayout, a VQA dataset of ~20K real-world multi-layer layouts annotated with compositional element pairs and design intent. Through empirical analysis on CoDeLayout, we identify two key challenges for existing VLMs: semantic drift between textual metadata and visual content, and structural ambiguity in hierarchical inter-element relationships. To address these challenges, we propose MASON, a post-training paradigm that integrates multimodal alignment (MA) and structural perception (SP). MA enhances element interpretation by grounding metadata-defined elements to their visual counterparts, mitigating semantic drift, while SP models layer-aware inter-element spatial relationships to improve hierarchical understanding and reduce structural ambiguity. Experiments reveal substantial gaps in existing VLMs: even the strongest baseline, GPT-o3, achieves only 79.68% accuracy, whereas Qwen2.5-VL 7B with MASON reaches 91.66%. Notably, MASON surpasses full-data Direct Finetune using only 30% of the training data and scales better with additional data.

</details>


### [32] [Parameter Efficient Continual Learning for Sparse Event-Based Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26720)
*Vaishnavi Nagabhushana,Kartikay Agrawal,Ayon Borthakur*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: sLoTh proposes a parameter-efficient continual learning framework for pretrained sparse event-based vision transformers, freezing the backbone and enabling adaptation via low-rank attention updates (seLoRA) and shared neuronal threshold modulation, updating <1% of parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: to enable continual learning in energy-efficient sparse event-based ViTs under memory/energy constraints without replay, addressing catastrophic forgetting while preserving efficiency.

Method: freeze backbone; apply scalable-efficient low-rank attention updates (seLoRA) and shared neuronal threshold modulation; no rehearsal buffers; updates <1% of parameters; evaluated on CIFAR-100, Tiny-ImageNet, ImageNet-100, ImageNet-R with up to 100 tasks.

Result: competitive rehearsal-free performance in class-incremental and online continual learning; ~6.5x energy reduction compared to dense ViTs.

Conclusion: demonstrates that parameter-efficient continual learning is achievable for sparse event-based ViTs, enabling adaptation with minimal plasticity and energy consumption while maintaining performance.

Abstract: Robotic and edge intelligence systems operate in dynamic environments where data arrives continuously, requiring models to adapt while preserving previously learned knowledge under strict memory and energy constraints. While parameter-efficient fine-tuning has shown promise for continual learning with vision transformers, conventional architectures rely on dense computation and remain costly for real-world deployment. Sparse event-based vision transformers provide energy-efficient event-driven computation, yet their continual learning capabilities remain largely unexplored. We here introduce sLoTh, a parameter-efficient continual learning framework for pretrained sparse event-based (spiking) vision transformers. sLoTh freezes the backbone and restricts plasticity to scalable-efficient low-rank attention updates (seLoRA) and shared neuronal threshold modulation, enabling adaptation without replay buffers by updating less than 1% of model parameters. Experiments across CIFAR-100, Tiny-ImageNet, ImageNet-100, and ImageNet-R with up to 100 tasks demonstrate competitive rehearsal-free performance in class-incremental learning and online continual learning, while enabling approximately 6.5x lower energy consumption than conventional dense vision transformers.

</details>


### [33] [UniGeo: A Multi-modal Large Language Model for Text-Guided Cross-View Geo-Localization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26722)
*Jiahao Wen,Hang Yu,Zhedong Zheng*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: UniGeo introduces a unified multimodal LLM for text-guided drone geo-localization, achieving fine-grained matching by geo-semantic understanding, cross-view generation, and candidate verification, with multi-stage training and strong gains on GeoText-1652.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Global cross-modal matching is insufficient for fine-grained localization due to incomplete queries and highly similar candidates; bridging drone–satellite semantics and enabling candidate-level discrimination is needed.

Method: A shared vision-language framework enabling (1) geo-semantic learning to align local scene elements and relations with language, (2) cross-view generation to map drone and satellite views, and (3) a plug-and-play verification module for candidate discrimination; training proceeds in multiple stages to progressively learn these components.

Result:  achieves consistent improvements across backbones; on GeoText-1652, R@10 increases by 13.59 percentage points and mAP by 2.83 points.

Conclusion: UniGeo effectively enhances fine-grained text-guided drone geo-localization by integrating geo-semantic understanding, cross-view generation, and candidate verification within a unified framework and staged training.

Abstract: Text-guided drone geo-localization aims to identify a target region in a large-scale image gallery from a natural-language description. Existing methods mainly formulate this task as direct matching between an open-ended text query and candidate images. However, incomplete queries and highly similar candidates often make global cross-modal matching insufficient for reliable fine-grained localization. We propose UniGeo, a unified multimodal large language model (MLLM) for text-guided drone geo-localization. Built on a shared vision-language framework, UniGeo jointly supports geo-semantic understanding, cross-view semantic generation, and candidate-level verification. Specifically, it establishes stable correspondences among local scene elements, spatial relations, and language descriptions through geo-semantic learning, and further models semantic mappings between drone and satellite views through cross-view generation. Based on these capabilities, a plug-and-play verification module performs fine-grained discrimination among highly confusable candidates. We further introduce a multi-stage training strategy that progressively learns geo-semantic understanding, cross-view generation, and candidate verification, improving adaptation to text-guided geo-localization. Experiments demonstrate consistent improvements across multiple retrieval backbones. On GeoText-1652, UniGeo improves R@10 and mAP by 13.59 and 2.83 percentage points, respectively, validating its effectiveness for fine-grained text-guided drone geo-localization.

</details>


### [34] [GeoMAD: Geometry-Aware Multi-View Anomaly Detection via Deformable Fusion and Distributional Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26724)
*Shang-Fu Chen,Jhih-Ciang Wu,Kuan-Chuan Peng,Wen-Huang Cheng,Kai-Lung Hua*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: GeoMAD is a unified multi-view anomaly detection framework that combines cross-view deformable fusion and distributional view alignment to enable geometry-aware, scalable 2D feature fusion without 3D supervision, achieving strong results on Real-IAD and MANTA-Tiny.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The main challenge is to fuse complementary observations from multiple camera viewpoints with geometric awareness at scale for multi-class industrial anomaly detection. Existing voxel-based fusion yields explicit geometry but is costly and class-specific, while patch-based fusion is efficient but relies on discrete matching and lacks continuous cross-view correspondence.

Method: GeoMAD introduces (1) Cross-view Deformable Fusion Module (CDFM) that learns content-adaptive, view-pair-specific sampling offsets on 2D feature maps and aggregates them across a multi-scale window pyramid with a global reference, enabling geometry-aware cross-view fusion without camera calibration, voxel construction, or 3D supervision; (2) Distributional View Alignment (DVA), a self-supervised loss that aligns each view's bottleneck distribution to a per-instance view-centric target, enforcing global distributional consistency without pixel-level correspondence.

Result: Extensive experiments on Real-IAD and MANTA-Tiny show strong detection and localization performance for unified multi-view anomaly detection.

Conclusion: GeoMAD bridges local geometric correspondence with global distributional consistency, delivering geometry-aware and distribution-consistent fusion while preserving the efficiency of 2D feature-space learning, validated by strong results on Real-IAD and MANTA-Tiny.

Abstract: Multi-view anomaly detection (MvAD) detects defects by exploiting complementary observations from multiple camera viewpoints. The central challenge is to fuse views with sufficient geometric awareness while remaining scalable to multi-class industrial settings. Existing methods typically fall into two extremes: voxel-based fusion provides explicit geometric alignment but requires costly 3D construction and class-specific assumptions, whereas lightweight patch-based fusion is efficient but relies on discrete candidate matching and lacks continuous cross-view correspondence. In this paper, we propose GeoMAD, a unified multi-view, multi-class AD framework that addresses both geometric correspondence deficiency and distributional inconsistency. Our \textit{Cross-view Deformable Fusion Module} (CDFM) learns content-adaptive, view-pair-specific sampling offsets directly on 2D feature maps and arranges them across a multi-scale window pyramid with image-global reference sampling, enabling hierarchical cross-view correspondence without camera calibration, voxel construction, or class-specific 3D supervision. We further introduce \textit{Distributional View Alignment} (DVA), a self-supervised cross-view regularization loss that aligns each view's bottleneck distribution against a per-instance view-centric target, enforcing global consistency without pixel-level correspondence. Together, CDFM and DVA bridge local geometric correspondence and global distributional consistency, providing geometry-aware and distribution-consistent fusion while preserving the efficiency of 2D feature-space learning. Extensive experiments on Real-IAD and MANTA-Tiny show that GeoMAD achieves strong detection and localization performance in unified MvAD.

</details>


### [35] [Generative Semantic Scene Completion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26737)
*Shi Chen,Weifeng Ge*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Discrete-diffusion-based generative framework for outdoor LiDAR SSC with PS^3, SGSC, and S^2D^2; achieves state-of-the-art mIoU on SemanticKITTI under strict single-sweep, single-sample constraint.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Outdoor SSC remains severely imbalanced and sparse; deterministic completion limits realism; a generative, data-efficient approach can better model uncertain, long-tail semantic completions and leverage paired sparse-dense data.

Method: Introduce PS^3 to synthesize paired sparse observations and dense completions (PS^3-SemanticKITTI); SGSC uses multinomial discrete diffusion conditioned on BEV semantic map and a sparse 3D feature stream to generate a scene from noise; S^2D^2 performs a single flow-matching refinement for existing completions; no base retraining; 1-step diffusion yields competitive mIoU; 4-step corrections with 8-view TT augmentations boost results.

Result: 1-step SGSC without TT achieves 38.8% mIoU on SemanticKITTI hidden test, +2.1 percentage points over prior best under same restriction; 4 correction steps with 8-view TT reach 39.2%.

Conclusion: Demonstrates that discrete diffusion-based generative SSC can surpass strong baselines under strict inference constraints and provide strong baseline for long-tail outdoor semantic completion; the PS^3 corpus complements existing datasets and enables improved training.

Abstract: Outdoor LiDAR semantic scene completion (SSC) recovers a dense semantic voxel grid from a scan observing 1% of the target volume, under class imbalance beyond 7,000x. We recast SSC as generative semantic scene completion (GSSC): a single discrete-diffusion formulation in three roles. First, paired sparse-dense scene synthesis (PS$^3$) generates matched sparse LiDAR observations with their dense semantic completions, addressing the long tail at its source and yielding the PS$^3$-SemanticKITTI corpus we train on alongside SemanticKITTI. Second, semantic-guided generative scene completion (SGSC) generates the scene from noise with multinomial discrete diffusion, conditioned on the sparse scan through a bird's-eye-view semantic map and a sparse 3D feature stream. Third, the same framework instead refines an existing completion in one flow-matching step: structured source discrete diffusion (S$^2$D$^2$). S$^2$D$^2$ improves the mIoU of SGSC's own output and every external SSC base tested, without base retraining or test-time adaptation. On the strongest base, one step without test-time augmentation reaches 38.8% mIoU on the SemanticKITTI hidden test. To our knowledge that is the best causal, single-sweep, single-sample result on that leaderboard, +2.1 pp over the previous best published score under the same restriction. Four correction steps with eight-view test-time augmentation reach 39.2%, outside that restriction.

</details>


### [36] [G2D: Generative-to-Discriminative Collaborative Inference for Zero-Shot Image Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26744)
*Zehua Hao,Fang Liu,Qinliang Wang,Yaoyang Du,Xinyan Huang,Puhua Chen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: G2D splits zero-shot classification into broad candidate retrieval and fine-grained verification using a training-free generative VLM to verify CLIP-retrieved candidates, improving accuracy over CLIP and standalone VLMs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Discriminative models (e.g., CLIP) suffer from top-1 mispredictions while keeping correct labels in top-K, whereas standalone generative models struggle with large label spaces. A method that separates rough candidate retrieval from precise image-grounded reasoning can combine the strengths of both.

Method: G2D adopts a training-free framework that uses a generative VLM to verify CLIP-retrieved candidates against the image. It uses candidate names and CLIP probabilities as priors, and employs fixed confidence routing, entropy-adaptive candidate sizing, and trie-constrained decoding to produce exactly one valid output per input at test time.

Result: On eight benchmarks, G2D achieves 68.85% average accuracy (vs 59.35% for CLIP and 63.11% for the standalone VLM). Across seven generator configurations, candidate-set verification yields a 1.08–27.42 point accuracy gain. G2D also transfers to DCLIP, WaffleCLIP, and CuPL, demonstrating practical interoperability; code available at the provided GitHub link.

Conclusion: Separating broad candidate retrieval from image-grounded verification enables effective, training-free enhancement of zero-shot classification by leveraging complementary strengths of discriminative and generative VLMs, with broad applicability across related models.

Abstract: Zero-shot classification needs efficient label retrieval and fine-grained visual reasoning, yet discriminative and generative vision-language models fail in complementary ways.When CLIP's top-1 prediction is wrong, the correct label often remains in its top-$K$ shortlist, making disambiguation rather than recall the key challenge.Standalone generative models, however, are hindered by large label spaces and unconstrained outputs.This complementarity motivates separating broad candidate retrieval from fine-grained, image-grounded verification.We propose G2D, a training-free framework that uses a generative VLM to verify CLIP-retrieved candidates against the image.Candidate names and CLIP probabilities provide a structured prior for resolving visually similar classes.Fixed confidence routing, entropy-adaptive candidate sizing, and trie-constrained decoding focus generative reasoning on uncertain samples and ensure one valid output for each input at test time.Across eight benchmarks, G2D achieves 68.85% average accuracy, versus 59.35% for CLIP and 63.11% for the standalone VLM.Across seven generator configurations, candidate-set verification improves average accuracy by 1.08--27.42 percentage points.G2D also transfers to DCLIP, WaffleCLIP, and CuPL, supporting a practical interface between discriminative proposal and generative visual reasoning. Code: https://github.com/Harzva/G2D

</details>


### [37] [CGS-SLAM: Collaborative Gaussian Splatting based SLAM for Multi-Agent Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26868)
*Jean-Daniel de Ambrogi,Aladine Chetouani,Vincent Nguyen,Aurélien Chateigner*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Hybrid RGB+IMU multi-agent SLAM enabling 3D scene reconstruction with a monocular depth estimator, sharing keyframes for submap alignment, and central server fusion to achieve low communication and robust GNSS-denied performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: RGB-D-based SLAM limitations on consumer devices and lack of collaborative 3D Generative Scene SLAM (3DGS).

Method: Hybrid decentralized/centralized system: each agent performs local tracking with IMU priors and reconstructs a scaled map using a metric monocular depth estimator (Depth Pro). Keyframe encodings are shared to enable dynamic keyframing in overlapping regions. A central server aligns submaps using VGGT as a view alignment model; bidirectional communication keeps mapping cost low while enabling global reconstruction.

Result: Experiments on multiple datasets show competitive tracking, improved rendering quality over state-of-the-art, and accurate submap alignment.

Conclusion: Demonstrates feasibility of RGB+IMU multi-agent 3DGS SLAM with low communication overhead and GNSS-denied robustness, enabling higher-fidelity rendering and scalable collaborative mapping.

Abstract: Recent advances in SLAM have leveraged 3DGS for photorealistic reconstruction and novel view synthesis. However, most methods rely on RGB-D input, which is unavailable on consumer-grade smartphones, and few integrate 3DGS within a collaborative framework. Therefore, we present CGS-SLAM, a hybrid decentralized/centralized system enabling multi-agent 3DGS SLAM using only RGB and inertial data. Each agent performs local tracking with inertial data as a motion prior and reconstructs a scaled map using a metric monocular depth estimator (Depth Pro). Keyframe encodings are shared among agents, enabling dynamic keyframing in regions of spatial overlaps with other agents, enhancing submap alignment. Afterwards, a central server aligns submaps using VGGT as a view alignment model. This bidirectional communication keeps communication cost low during mapping and global reconstruction in difficult GNSS-denied environments. Experiments on multiple datasets demonstrate competitive tracking performance, improved rendering quality over state-of-the-art methods, and accurate submap alignment.

</details>


### [38] [Glass Surface Detection Grounded in 3D Visual Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26752)
*Yiwei Lu,Ke Xu,Tao Yan,Xiaojun Chang,Radu Timofte,Rynson W. H. Lau*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Proposes grounding glass surface detection in 3D geometry using VGGT-derived priors, introducing FSAM and GeGB within a multi-task framework to achieve state-of-the-art results across multiple benchmarks and improved scene reconstruction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 2D appearance cues are often unreliable for glass due to transparency and reflection; leveraging 3D geometry can explicitly model the physical existence of glass surfaces and improve detection and reconstruction.

Method: Distill 3D priors from the visual geometry grounded transformer (VGGT) to form glass-aware 3D representations. Introduce a multi-task glass detection head with two modules: (1) Frequency Self-Attention Module (FSAM) for glass-specific spectral features; (2) Geometry Grounding Block (GeGB) that selectively grounds 2D features in 3D geometry for segmentation.

Result: Achieves state-of-the-art performance on seven standard GSD benchmarks, demonstrates strong generalization to video and multi-modal data, and substantially improves reconstruction quality in scenes with glass.

Conclusion: Grounding GSD in 3D visual geometry via VGGT priors and specialized modules (FSAM, GeGB) is an effective strategy for handling transparent/reflective surfaces, enabling robust detection and better scene understanding; code is available.

Abstract: Glass surface detection (GSD) is critical for scene understanding and reconstruction, and yet remains challenging due to the transparency and reflectivity of glass surfaces. Existing GSD methods typically rely on 2D appearance cues, which may fail in geometrically ambiguous scenes. In this paper, we propose a paradigm shift: grounding GSD in 3D visual geometry to explicitly model the physical existence of glass surfaces. Our method first distills rich 3D priors from the visual geometry grounded transformer (VGGT) and generates glass-aware 3D representations. It then exploits multi-tasking learning with a novel glass detection head, consisting of two core modules: a Frequency Self-Attention Module (FSAM) that identifies glass-specific spectral features for glass surface localization, and a Geometry Grounding Block (GeGB) that selectively grounds 2D features in 3D geometry for glass surface segmentation. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method achieves state-of-the-art performance across seven standard GSD benchmarks, generalizes well to video/multi-modal data, and substantially improves reconstruction in glass scenes. Code is available in https://github.com/YT3DVision/VGGT_GLASS.

</details>


### [39] [SpatialCrafter: Single Image World Modeling with Generative 3D Proxies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27073)
*Chuan Fang,Lingteng Qiu,Yixun Liang,Rui Chen,Kunming Luo,Zhaohua Zheng,Tongyuan Bai,Feipeng Tian,Zilong Dong,Zihan Zhou,Ping Tan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Introduces SpatialCrafter, a two-stage image-to-scene generator using a global 3D proxy and a refinement stage, addressing drift and 3D consistency in VDM-based methods; includes a novel PaSS Flow proxy, training strategies, and a 115K-scene dataset; shows improved performance and robustness.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Explorable image-to-scene generation with stable, high-fidelity 3D consistency is challenging when relying on incomplete signals (sparse point clouds, 2D panoramas), leading to hallucinations and drift.

Method: A two-stage framework: (1) global proxy generation via PaSS Flow to produce a geometrically consistent 3D proxy, and (2) appearance refinement via a Generative Deferred Refiner that adds high-frequency details on proxy-defined geometry. Training includes Parallel Geometry Injection and Proxy-Aware Corruption to integrate proxies with pretrained VDMs. A new 115K-scene dataset is constructed.

Result: SpatialCrafter outperforms state-of-the-art methods, reduces long-term drift, and remains robust under rapid camera motion and extreme viewpoint changes. The authors also release code, models, and the new dataset.

Conclusion: By coupling a learnable 3D proxy with pretrained diffusion models through targeted training strategies, SpatialCrafter achieves high-fidelity, explorable image-to-scene generation with improved stability and 3D consistency and provides a new large-scale dataset for this task.

Abstract: Explorable image-to-scene generation is essential for applications in gaming, robotics, and virtual reality. Existing methods based on video diffusion model (VDM) commonly rely on incomplete conditioning signals such as sparse point clouds or 2D panoramas, leading to stochastic hallucinations, long-term drifts and suboptimal 3D consistency. We present SpatialCrafter, a novel two-stage framework that addresses these issues by introducing a global 3D proxy for high-fidelity image-to-scene generation. Specifically, we decompose the generation process into global proxy generation and appearance refinement. For proxy generation, we propose a Point-anchored Sparse Structure~(PaSS) Flow module that predicts a spatially aligned and geometrically consistent 3D proxy. For appearance refinement, we re-frame the VDM as a Generative Deferred Refiner which synthesizes high-frequency photorealistic details upon proxy-defined scene geometry. To better integrate the proxy with the pre-trained VDM, we introduce Parallel Geometry Injection and Proxy-Aware Corruption training strategies, which improve robustness to proxy artifacts without disrupting the pretrained generative manifold. Furthermore, as no suitable dataset exists for this explorable scene generation task, we construct a new large-scale dataset of 115K scenes. To the best of our knowledge, it is the first hybrid dataset for image-to-scene generation. Extensive experiments on both synthetic and real-world datasets show that SpatialCrafter outperforms state-of-the-art methods, mitigates long-term drift, and remains robust and consistent under rapid camera motion and extreme viewpoint changes. Code, models, and the newly constructed dataset will be publicly released. See more at https://fangchuan.github.io/SpatialCrafter/.

</details>


### [40] [Cross-Architecture Knowledge Distillation from a Vision Foundation Model to a Lightweight Visual State Space Model for Tea Leaf Disease Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26771)
*Zibo Zhou,Zongsen Qiu,Rui Chen,Yujie Yao,Yue Zhou,Jianjun Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Cross-architecture knowledge distillation from a fine-tuned DINOv2 ViT teacher to a compact LVSSM student yields significant edge-deployment gains for tea leaf disease classification, achieving ~3 percentage points higher accuracy with 5x fewer parameters, though results are dataset-limited.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Enable accurate leaf-disease classification on edge devices with tight compute budgets by leveraging a large, powerful teacher model to train a small, efficient student across architectures.

Method: Fine-tune a DINOv2 Vision Transformer as a teacher and perform KD to a 4.45M-parameter LVSSM student. Address training stability issues (one large patch-embedding conv and a residual-severing fusion layer) with a progressive convolutional stem and a gated bidirectional selective-scan block. Use temperature-scaled logit distillation; compare to intermediate feature-alignment losses; evaluate with multiple seeds and report per-class metrics, confusion matrices, bootstrap CIs, and FLOPs/latency.

Result: The LVSSM student achieved 95.41% test accuracy on tea leaf disease classification (best run 96.20%), macro-F1 94.45%, versus 92.32±2.14% for the initial baseline; gains of +3.09 percentage points (mean). The student has 5.0x fewer parameters than the 22M-parameter teacher yet retains 98.3% of its accuracy. Ablations suggest simple logit-level KD is strongest; intermediate feature-alignment losses degrade accuracy. Fair-from-scratch comparisons indicate the gain is specific to students starting below the teacher. Reported metrics include per-class metrics, confusion matrices, bootstrap CIs, FLOPs/latency; limitations include single-dataset scope and a simplified non-official SSM implementation.

Conclusion: Cross-architecture KD from a large ViT teacher to a compact LVSSM student is effective for edge deployment in agriculture when the student begins with lower capacity than the teacher. The proposed architectural fixes enable stable training on limited data. Limitations suggest validating on broader datasets and with official SSM implementations; future work could explore generalization and real-world latency on edge hardware.

Abstract: Automated tea leaf disease classification supports precision agriculture, yet deploying accurate models on edge devices remains challenging under tight compute budgets. Self-supervised vision foundation models such as DINOv2 provide strong features but are too large for field deployment, while lightweight models trained from scratch on small agricultural datasets often underfit. We study cross-architecture knowledge distillation (KD) from a fine-tuned DINOv2 teacher (Vision Transformer) to a compact bidirectional Visual State Space Model (LVSSM) student, an underexplored direction because the architectures use fundamentally different token-mixing mechanisms. We identify and fix two training-stability problems that prevent the from-scratch SSM student from learning on limited data: a single large patch-embedding convolution and a fusion layer that severs the residual path. With a progressive convolutional stem and gated bidirectional selective-scan block, the 4.45M-parameter student trains stably. Across three seeds, temperature-scaled logit distillation raises test accuracy from 92.32+/-2.14% to 95.41+/-1.17% (best single run: 96.20%; macro-F1: 94.45%), a +3.09 percentage-point mean gain. The student uses 5.0 times fewer parameters than the 22M-parameter teacher while retaining 98.3% of its accuracy. Ablations show that intermediate feature-alignment losses reduce accuracy, making simple logit-level KD the strongest configuration. A fair from-scratch comparison shows the gain is specific to students that start below the teacher. We report per-class metrics, confusion matrices, bootstrap confidence intervals, and FLOPs/latency measurements, and discuss limitations including the single-dataset scope and simplified non-official SSM implementation.

</details>


### [41] [TADP: Task-Aware Deformable Prediction for Single-Stage 3D Object Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27282)
*Su Wang,Yaochen Li,Min Yang,Jiaohao Nie,Chang Liu,Yuehu Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Task-aware deformable prediction (TADP) for single-stage 3D object detection, with triple feature refinement, multi-scale fusion, and deformable heads, achieving KITTI car mAP 80.91%.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Single-stage detectors share a common feature for all tasks, which cannot be universally optimal; introduce task-aware deformation to tailor features to each task’s emphasis and interactions.

Method: Propose TADP: (1) triple feature refinement aggregation to extract three-level features adaptively; (2) multi-scale feature aggregation block for scale-aware fusion; (3) plug-and-play task-aware deformation heads for each task, with three deformation modules to model emphasis and interaction.

Result: On KITTI, car mAP reaches 80.91%, surpassing many state-of-the-art methods; deformation head also improves other detectors.

Conclusion: Task-aware deformation heads effectively capture per-task emphasis and cross-task interactions, improving performance of single-stage 3D detectors across architectures.

Abstract: Most single-stage 3D object detectors complete different tasks with the same extracted features. Nevertheless, it is impossible to project features into a common space that is adaptive for all the tasks. We present a novel task-aware deformable prediction (TADP) method for single-stage 3D object detection to solve this problem. Firstly, a triple feature refinement aggregation module is designed to extract three-level features adaptively. Additionally, we design the multi-scale feature aggregation block to fuse multi-scale features in a scale-aware manner. Finally, the prediction of each task is deformed with the designed plug-and-play task-aware deformation head. It can percept the emphasis and interaction of each task. We also designed three different deformation modules. The experimental results demonstrate that the proposed deformation head shows good results on other detection methods. The experimental results on the KITTI dataset demonstrate that the car mAP is 80.91%, surpassing many state-of-the-art methods on the KITTI benchmark.

</details>


### [42] [Ring Forcing: Towards Precise Long-Term Memory for Autoregressive Video Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26794)
*Bowen Xue,Brandon Y. Feng,Chenguo Lin,Yuchen Lin,Yujia Zeng,Lvmin Zhang,Maneesh Agrawala,Honglei Yan,Panwang Pan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Ring Forcing introduces a ring-structured autoregressive video diffusion model to scale video generation with robust long-term memory, achieving minutes-long coherence and improved object permanence by combining distant-history retrieval, memory compression, and scalable memory adaptation via sparse RoPE.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Scaling video generation to long durations is bottlenecked by a lack of robust long-term memory. Effective long-term memory requires both object permanence (reproducing object identity/pappearance across re-entries) and memory capacity (handling ultra-long context).

Method: Ring Forcing uses ring-structured training to enforce retrieval from distant history, a compression and timestep composition strategy to expand memory within fixed sequence lengths, and a sparse RoPE mechanism to enable flexible, scalable memory adaptation while leveraging pre-trained priors.

Result: Extensive experiments show minutes-long coherence and object permanence, significantly outperforming state-of-the-art methods.

Conclusion: Ring Forcing reconciles strict historical adherence with generative diversity, expanding memory capacity to minutes-long durations and providing robust long-term memory for video generation.

Abstract: Scaling video generation to long durations reveals a critical bottleneck: current models lack robust long-term memory. This deficiency can be studied along two critical aspects: object permanence, the ability to precisely reproduce the appearance of objects upon re-entry; and memory capacity, the ability to process ultra-long context and use information from distant history. Robust long-term memory requires both: object permanence without sufficient context handling limits the temporal scope, while long context length without permanence fails to maintain identity. To address this, we present Ring Forcing, an autoregressive video diffusion framework designed to robustly construct and precisely utilize long-term memory. Our ring-structured training strategy enforces retrieval from distant history, effectively reconciling the trade-off between strict historical adherence and generative diversity. To expand memory capacity, we introduce a compression and timestep composition strategy. Under fixed sequence length constraints, this method extends the effective historical span to minutes-long durations and achieves a comprehensive receptive field over the entire history. Furthermore, we present a sparse RoPE mechanism to enable flexible, scalable memory adaptation while fully exploiting pre-trained priors. Extensive experiments demonstrate that Ring Forcing achieves superior minutes-long coherence and object permanence, significantly outperforming state-of-the-art methods.

</details>


### [43] [Multi-Image Visual Token Pruning in Large Visual Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26806)
*Rongyang Zhang,Chengqiang Lu,Cong Li,Hongchao Gu,Tingjia Shen,Xuyang Zhi,Qimeng Wang,Yan Gao,Yi Wu,Yao Hu,Hao Wang,Enhong Chen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Training-free Adaptive Visual Token Pruning (AVTP) for diverse LVLMs with multi-image inputs, enabling adaptive token budgets and selective pruning layers to achieve substantial speedups while preserving accuracy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing visual token pruning methods rely on static strategies tied to specific architectures and attention computations, limiting generality, efficiency (e.g., with FlashAttention), and applicability to multi-image scenarios.

Method: Empirically analyze visual attention distributions across multiple LVLMs to select pruning layers; apply adaptive pruning ratios per image so more important images retain more tokens; train-free, compatible with varied LVLM architectures and multi-image inputs.

Result: Across several LVLMs, AVTP yields 2x inference speedup for Qwen3VL-8B with 96.1% of original accuracy on multi-image benchmarks; InternVL3.5-8B preserves 94.1% accuracy; LLaVA-OV-7B outperforms its baseline. Code released at GitHub.


Conclusion: AVTP provides a robust, architecture-agnostic, training-free visual token pruning framework that improves efficiency in multi-image LVLMs with minimal or even positive impact on accuracy.

Abstract: With the growing demand for processing multiple image sequences in real-world applications, various visual token pruning methods have emerged to mitigate the computational and context length constraints faced by Large Vision Language Models (LVLMs). However, most existing pruning approaches rely on static strategies that struggle to adapt across different architectural LVLMs and multi-image scenarios, and are additionally constrained by their dependence on attention computations that are incompatible with efficient techniques like FlashAttention. To address these limitations, we propose a training-free, Adaptive Visual Token Pruning (AVTP) framework, applicable to diverse LVLM architectures. We strategically determine pruning layers based on empirical analysis of visual attention distributions across various LVLMs, and implement adaptive pruning ratios in multi-image contexts where images of higher importance retain proportionally more tokens. We conduct extensive experiments across different LVLMs to demonstrate the effectiveness and robustness of AVTP. Specifically, Qwen3VL-8B achieves 2 times inference speedup while maintaining 96.1\% of its original accuracy on multiple multi-image benchmarks, InternVL3.5-8B retains 94.1\% accuracy, and LLaVA-OV-7B even exceeds its original baseline performance. Our code is available at \href{https://github.com/zry13/AVTP}{this link}.

</details>


### [44] [Thinking on Shots: Consistent Multi-Shot Video Editing with Agentic Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26809)
*Chenyang Wu,Fuchen Long,Binyuan Huang,Xinlong Sun,Xi Chen,Chun-Le Guo,Chongyi Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: We propose MMLVE: a long-video editing paradigm using LLMs and VLMs to achieve cross-shot consistency, multi-instruction decoupling, and zero-destruction on spatiotemporal structure; introduces MMLVE-Bench benchmark and analysis showing agentic editing outperforming Seedance 2.0 with fewer hallucinations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Long videos with multiple instructions lack cohesive editing. Naive chunking causes entity fragmentation, hallucinations, and disrupted temporal continuity; need cross-shot consistency and precise multi-instruction decoding for long-form video editing.

Method: Agentic editing framework that leverages the synergy of Large Language Models (LLMs) and Vision-Language Models (VLMs) to enable shot-level video decoupling and precise instruction parsing, achieving cross-shot consistency and zero-destruction on spatiotemporal structure. Introduces MMLVE-Bench dataset and three evaluation metrics tailored to MMLVE.

Result: MMLVE-Agent outperforms existing closed-source SOTA approaches (e.g., Seedance 2.0), eliminates editing hallucinations, preserves cross-shot editing consistency, and attains seamless spatiotemporal transitions across long videos.

Conclusion: The work presents a viable long-video editing paradigm that integrates LLMs and VLMs into an agentic, shot-wise editing framework, supported by a dedicated benchmark and metrics; it sets a new direction for robust, instruction-rich long-video editing.

Abstract: While generative AI has significantly advanced video editing, existing methods primarily focus on single-shot or short video clips. Editing long videos with multiple instructions remains a formidable challenge. Naive chunking strategies, e.g., fixed-duration segmentation, often lead to entity fragmentation, severe editing hallucinations, and disrupted temporal continuity. To bridge this gap, we introduce the Multi-Instruction Multi-Shot Long-Video Editing (MMLVE) task, which is structured around three core objectives: Cross-Shot Editing Consistency (CSEC), Multi-Instruction Decoupling (MID), and Zero-Destruction on Spatiotemporal Structure (ZDSS). To tackle these three unique challenges, we introduce an agentic editing framework that leverages the synergy of Large Language Models (LLMs) and Vision-Language Models (VLMs) to achieve shot-level video decoupling and precise instruction parsing. Furthermore, to comprehensively evaluate this task, we construct MMLVE-Bench, which is an MMLVE-focused dataset characterized by complex real-world spatiotemporal dynamics, high-density heterogeneous instructions, and sparse, random entity distributions. Three MMLVE-focused evaluation metrics are further exploited to assess the quality of the editing results. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our MMLVE-Agent outperforms existing closed-source SOTA approaches (e.g., Seedance 2.0), successfully eliminating editing hallucinations, preserving cross-shot editing consistency, and attaining seamless spatiotemporal transitions.

</details>


### [45] [Hyperspectral Diffusion Equivariant Imaging (HyDiff-EI): A Self-supervised Framework for Hyperspectral Image Inpainting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26812)
*Shuo Li,Mike Davies,Mehrdad Yaghoobi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A test-time optimization diffusion framework, HyDiff-EI, for hyperspectral image inpainting that uses equivariant priors without large pretraining, improving robustness and generalization across real datasets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: HSI inpainting is ill-posed and data-scarce; there is a need for sensor-agnostic, data-efficient restoration that leverages physics-informed priors; large annotated HSI datasets are limited.

Method: HyDiff-EI performs unsupervised, test-time optimization on a single corrupted HSI; embeds equivariant consistency constraints within the diffusion process; exploits geometric symmetries and intrinsic HSIs; no large-scale pretraining; self-supervised and sensor-flexible.

Result: Experiments on real datasets (Chikusei, Botswana, EMIT) show superior inpainting quality to existing self-supervised and diffusion-based baselines in both noiseless and noisy scenarios; improved noise robustness and generalizability.

Conclusion: Demonstrates a practical integration of diffusion modeling with physical priors for HSI inpainting, enabling flexible, sensor-aware restoration in data-limited remote sensing contexts.

Abstract: A novel Hyperspectral diffusion Equivariant Imaging (HyDiff-EI) framework for solving the hyperspectral image (HSI) inpainting problem has been presented here. Unlike conventional diffusion-based methods that rely on large-scale pretraining, HyDiff-EI is a test-time optimization framework that learns directly from a single corrupted HSI acquisition. This makes it flexible for different sensor configurations and particularly well-suited for practical remote sensing scenarios where large annotated hyperspectral datasets are limited. To address the ill-posed nature of unsupervised inpainting, we embed equivariant consistency constraints within the diffusion process. By leveraging the inherent geometric symmetries and intrinsic characteristics of HSIs, HyDiff-EI bridges the gap between generative diffusion modeling and self-consistent physical priors. We empirically show that coupling diffusion modeling with equivariant priors substantially enhances noise robustness and generalizability. Extensive experiments on real-world datasets including Chikusei, Botswana, and EMIT demonstrate that HyDiff-EI offers remarkable inpainting quality over existing self-supervised and diffusion-based algorithms in both noiseless and noisy cases.

</details>


### [46] [LLaVAFlow: Preserving Latent Alignment Flow for Parameter-Efficient Multimodal Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26820)
*Muyao Yuan,Muyan Jiao,Jiangyong Ying,Weizhan Zhang,Yuanhong Zhang,Lan Ma,Yuan Gao,Haipeng Du*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: LLaVAFlow uses information-theoretic distillation to preserve cross-modal alignment flow in MLLMs during fine-tuning, improving downstream performance and generalization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Catastrophic forgetting during visual instruction tuning degrades generalization; cross-modal alignment is crucial but underexplored, and often implicitly captured in an information-flow trajectory.

Method: LLaVAFlow introduces an information-theoretic distillation framework: (1) compress mutual information between extracted relations and MLLM embeddings to shape a refined alignment flow via a learnable module; (2) maximize mutual information between alignment flows of pretrained and fine-tuned MLLMs to transfer compact alignment information; it is plug-and-play.

Result: Empirical results show improved downstream performance and generalization, with ablations validating the effectiveness of MI compression and cross-flow alignment preservation; demonstrated as a plug-and-play framework.

Conclusion: Preserving the alignment flow via information-theoretic distillation helps maintain cross-modal alignment during tuning, yielding better generalization for MLLMs; LLaVAFlow offers a practical method to mitigate forgetting.

Abstract: While Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) exhibit strong generalization, visual instruction tuning for downstream tasks inevitably causes catastrophic forgetting, impairing overall generalization. While existing methods regulate weight updates to reduce forgetting, they overlook the fundamental cross-modal alignment in MLLMs. Based on prior work and our observations, we argue that cross-modal alignment is implicitly captured in the information-compression trajectory. To preserve the alignment flow embedded in the trajectory, we propose LLaVAFlow, an information-theoretic distillation framework. First, we compress the mutual information between the extracted relations and MLLM embeddings, encouraging a learnable module to produce a refined alignment flow that benefits downstream tasks. Second, we maximize the mutual information between the extracted alignment flows of the pretrained and fine-tuned MLLMs, enabling the transfer of compact alignment information. Extensive experiments show that LLaVAFlow is an effective plug-and-play framework that preserves alignment flow and enhances both downstream performance and generalization.

</details>


### [47] [Evaluator-Dependent Patient-Adaptive ECG Lead-Channel Allocation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26827)
*Xiaoyang Li,Zeyan Tao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Evaluator dependence undermines transferability of learned channel-utility in patient-conditioned ECG lead acquisition; results show positive interaction across evaluators, suggesting adaptive sensing must be co-validated with the intended diagnostic backbone. Evaluator-aligned training helps but does not eliminate dependency, leaving joint sensing-diagnosis optimization as an open problem.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understand whether marginal gains from adaptive ECG channel selection generalize across diagnostic evaluators, addressing the risk that a policy optimized for one evaluator may underperform with another.

Method: Train two adaptive policies (ECG-on-Demand, MGA) with a controlled arbitrary-mask logistic evaluator. Freeze policies and evaluate on a held-out fold using a stronger masked raw-waveform ResNet1D. Use exhaustive search to create metric-matched population-wide fixed comparators per evaluator. Analyze interaction across budgets (k) and probabilistic metrics; perform three post-hoc sensitivity analyses (common-reference scoring, training evaluator on mixed masks, evaluator-aligned policy training).

Result: All 30 interaction estimates across two policies, five budgets, and three metrics are positive with confidence intervals excluding zero, indicating evaluator dependence of channel-utility. Evaluator-aligned training reduces the gap but does not fully eliminate it. Sensitivity analyses support the robustness of the positive interaction; findings persist across perturbations.

Conclusion: Adaptive channel allocation for ECG should be developed and validated together with its diagnostic backbone. Jointly optimized sensing-diagnosis systems remain an open problem; transferability of learned sensing policies across evaluators is limited without evaluator-aligned training.

Abstract: Patient-conditioned acquisition policies for ECG lead-channel selection can outperform population-wide fixed protocols by tailoring the channel budget to each patient's observed cardiac state. However, the value of acquiring any given channel is defined relative to a downstream diagnostic evaluator, so marginal utilities learned under one evaluator need not transfer when the evaluator is replaced. We study this evaluator dependence empirically on PTB-XL by freezing two policies (ECG-on-Demand and MGA) trained with a controlled arbitrary-mask logistic evaluator, then scoring their unchanged acquisition trajectories with a more predictive masked raw-waveform ResNet1D. Exhaustive search provides metric-matched population-wide fixed comparators separately for each evaluator, enabling a clean interaction contrast. At budget $k=4$ on a held-out evaluation fold, ECG-on-Demand shifts from $D_\mathrm{C}=-0.011$ (favoring adaptive under the controlled evaluator) to $D_\mathrm{S}=+0.029$ (favoring fixed under the strong evaluator), yielding an NLL interaction of $+0.041$ (95% CI $[+0.030, +0.050]$). Across two policies, five budgets, and three probabilistic metrics, all 30 interaction estimates are positive with paired confidence intervals excluding zero. Three post-hoc sensitivity analyses -- common-reference scoring, training the strong evaluator on a mixture of policy-generated and random masks, and evaluator-aligned Strong-MGA policy training -- each preserve a positive interaction interval, making reference-choice and mask-distribution artifacts less plausible explanations. Evaluator-aligned training reduces but does not eliminate the gap. These results indicate that adaptive ECG channel allocation should be developed and validated jointly with its intended diagnostic backbone, and that jointly optimized sensing-diagnosis systems remain an open problem.

</details>


### [48] [Rethinking Image Processing for the Age of AI: A Problem-First Framework for Scientific Progress](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26833)
*Guoping Qiu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Advocates a problem-first approach to imaging AI, separating the physical problem, solution principle, estimator, and implementation; introduces a six-stage workflow; argues benchmark-driven research can misalign with real-world problems; supports clearer evidence standards and cultural change, illustrated by super-resolution and low-light cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Counter the model-first, benchmark-driven research pattern that may yield impressive metrics without addressing underlying imaging problems; promote deeper understanding of imaging problems and genuine scientific/technical advancement.

Method: Proposes a six-stage workflow: 1) problem formulation and image acquisition, 2) information-loss analysis, 3) explicit assumptions, 4) ambiguity assessment, 5) evaluation strategy, before selecting models or datasets; uses case studies in super-resolution and low-light enhancement to show dataset-task misalignment; argues for clearer evidence, reproducibility, uncertainty reporting, and state-of-the-art claims.

Result: A conceptual framework and workflow that separate problem definition from modeling; identification of mismatches between benchmarks and real problems; proposed standards for evidence, reproducibility, uncertainty, and claims; practical guidance for conducting problem-first research in imaging with AI.

Conclusion: A shift toward a problem-first paradigm and improved research culture/education is needed to ensure AI advances solve actual imaging problems and that claims are robust and transferable; implementation hinges on adopting the six-stage workflow and the proposed standards.

Abstract: Modern AI has greatly expanded the capabilities of image processing. However, the ready availability of powerful models, public datasets, and benchmark leaderboards has also en- couraged a model-first research pattern: researchers increasingly begin with an available architecture and optimize it on a public benchmark, rather than beginning with the underlying real-world imaging problem. This can produce impressive benchmark results without necessarily improving our understanding or solution of the real problem. This paper argues for a problem-first approach that distinguishes the physical imaging problem, solution principle, statistical estimator, and computational implementation, while clarifying what modern AI can achieve and which fundamental problems remain unsolved. Through case studies of super- resolution and low-light enhancement, we show how benchmark datasets may define tasks that differ substantially from the real-world problems they are intended to represent, and why performance improvements must be interpreted within the conditions under which they are obtained. We propose a six-stage workflow that places problem formulation, image acquisition, information-loss analysis, assumptions, ambiguity, and evaluation before model and dataset selection. The paper also proposes clearer standards for evidence, reproducibility, uncertainty, and claims of state-of-the-art performance. More fundamentally, it calls for a change in research culture and education so that future researchers learn to understand imaging problems deeply and use modern AI to achieve genuine scientific and technical advancement.

</details>


### [49] [MedFG-VQA: Low-Frequency Memory and Graph Attention for Lightweight Medical VQA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26848)
*Haowen Gu,Gensheng Pei,Zeren Sun,Mingwu Ren,Xiangbo Shu,Yazhou Yao,Fumin Shen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MedFG-VQA introduces a lightweight, data-efficient medical VQA framework using a frequency-domain memory-augmented fusion (FMF) and graph-aware cross-attention (GACA), trained on a large synthetic dataset (SynMed-VQA) to achieve competitive performance with lower compute.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Medical VQA faces data scarcity and high computational demands of large vision-language models. A compact, efficient approach is needed for clinical deployment without sacrificing performance.

Method: Two core components: (1) Frequency-Memory Fusion (FMF) that enhances low-frequency image features via a learnable memory bank built on DCT decomposition; (2) Graph-Aware Cross-Attention (GACA) that aligns visual-textual representations through cross-attention followed by graph-convolutional refinement. A large synthetic dataset, SynMed-VQA, with >2M Q/A pairs across 9 imaging modalities and 10 organs is generated using GPT-4o to mitigate data scarcity. Evaluation on SynMed-VQA and three standard biomedical VQA benchmarks confirms competitive or superior performance relative to larger models, with substantially reduced computation.

Result: MedFG-VQA achieves competitive or superior accuracy compared to much larger models while requiring significantly less computation, indicating strong efficiency gains and potential for practical clinical deployment.

Conclusion: The combination of frequency-domain memory augmentation and graph-aware alignment yields strong VQA performance under data scarcity constraints. The synthetic SynMed-VQA dataset effectively supports training and evaluation. Future work may explore ablations, domain transfer, interpretability, and validation on real-world clinical data to establish robustness and generalizability.

Abstract: Medical Visual Question Answering (Med-VQA) holds significant promise for clinical decision support, yet faces challenges due to limited annotated data and the high computational demands of existing large vision-language models. We propose MedFG-VQA, a lightweight framework that leverages a memory bank to augment DCT-based low-frequency features and employs graph-enhanced cross-attention for effective visual-textual alignment. Specifically, our approach features two key components: Frequency-Memory Fusion (FMF), which enhances low-frequency features by retrieving from a learnable memory bank built on DCT decomposition, and Graph-Aware Cross-Attention (GACA), which aligns visual-textual features via cross-attention and refines them through graph-convolutional aggregation. To address data scarcity, we construct SynMed-VQA, a large-scale synthetic dataset comprising over 2 million question-answer pairs across 9 imaging modalities and 10 major organs, generated with GPT-4o. Extensive experiments on SynMed-VQA and three other standard biomedical VQA benchmarks demonstrate that MedFG-VQA achieves competitive or superior performance compared to much larger models while maintaining significantly lower computational costs, highlighting its efficiency and potential for clinical deployment.

</details>


### [50] [From Reasoning to Pixels: Grounded Medical Multimodal LLMs for VQA and Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26856)
*Haowen Gu,Gensheng Pei,Junzhu Mao,Qiong Wang,Mingwu Ren,Yazhou Yao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MedREAL introduces a reasoning-grounded framework for medical vision-language tasks that tightly couples linguistic reasoning with pixel-level localization. It presents Seg Anchored Reasoning Pooling (SARP) to distill task-relevant evidence from [SEG] tokens, and a Reasoning-to-Visual (R2V) fusion to feed this into a segmentation pipeline. It also provides MedRAVS-13K for evaluation. Empirical results show state-of-the-art performance on gIoU and cIoU (68.49% and 70.47%), with evidence masks fully consistent with textual diagnoses, enabling interpretable reasoning-driven analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current Multimodal LLMs for medical VQA rely on global image features, which hampers precise pixel-level grounding and undermines clinical trust. There is a need to bridge high-level reasoning with spatial localization.

Method: Propose Seg Anchored Reasoning Pooling (SARP) to extract task-relevant semantic cues from [SEG] tokens in the LLM hidden states. Introduce Reasoning-to-Visual (R2V) fusion to inject reasoning-aware features into a segmentation pipeline for accurate mask decoding. Compile MedRAVS-13K (13,824 samples across four imaging modalities) for evaluation.

Result: Outperforms state-of-the-art methods, achieving 68.49% gIoU and 70.47% cIoU on benchmark datasets. The method produces evidence masks that are strictly consistent with textual diagnoses, enhancing interpretability and trust.

Conclusion: MedREAL enables robust, interpretable medicine-specific reasoning by aligning linguistic reasoning with spatial grounding, through SARP and R2V, and provides a scalable dataset for Multimodal Medical VQA with segmentation grounding.

Abstract: Although Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have demonstrated impressive performance in Medical Visual Question Answering (Med-VQA), their reliance on global image features often lacks precise pixel-level grounding, thereby limiting clinical trustworthiness. To bridge the semantic gap between high-level clinical reasoning and spatial localization, we propose \textsc{\textsc{MedREAL}} (\textbf{Med}ical \textbf{RE}asoning-driven \textbf{A}nswering and \textbf{L}ocalization), a unified framework that seamlessly aligns linguistic reasoning with spatial grounding. Specifically, \textsc{MedREAL} introduces \textbf{S}eg \textbf{A}nchored \textbf{R}easoning \textbf{P}ooling (SARP) to distill task-relevant semantic evidence directly from \texttt{[SEG]} tokens within the MLLM's hidden states. Furthermore, a \textbf{R}easoning-to-\textbf{V}isual (R2V) fusion mechanism is proposed to effectively inject these reasoning-aware features into a segmentation pipeline for accurate mask decoding. To facilitate this paradigm, we construct MedRAVS-13K, a comprehensive dataset comprising 13,824 expertly validated samples across four diverse imaging modalities. Extensive experiments demonstrate that \textsc{MedREAL} significantly outperforms state-of-the-arts, achieving 68.49\% gIoU and 70.47\% cIoU on benchmark evaluations. By generating evidence masks that are strictly consistent with textual diagnoses, \textsc{MedREAL} provides a robust, interpretable framework for reasoning-driven medical image analysis.

</details>


### [51] [A Geometry-Driven, Framework-Agnostic Optimization for Object Pose Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26859)
*Wei Chen,Tao Zhen,Zhongchen Shi,Jing Zhang,Liang Xie,Erwei Yin*

Main category: cs.CV
