TL;DR: Data-centric pose estimation via principal-axes-aligned rotation representation; improves stability, resolves symmetry ambiguities at data level, and remains architecture-agnostic.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Model-centric focus dominates pose estimation research; propose geometry-driven, data-level optimization to reduce label noise, occlusion sensitivity, and rotational ambiguity by leveraging inertial properties.

Method: Define a rotation representation by aligning the object's coordinate system to its principal axes (inertia-derived). This yields energy-minimizing, stable rotations; implement symmetry-aware canonicalization to resolve ambiguities for symmetric objects at the data level; apply optimization purely at the dataset level with no architectural changes.

Result: Demonstrates consistent, significant accuracy improvements across both category-level and instance-level models while preserving baseline network integrity and compatibility.

Conclusion: Proposes a geometry-driven, plug-and-play data-centric direction for pose estimation that circumvents the need for complex network redesign.

Abstract: Current object pose estimation research remains predominantly model-centric, focusing on architectural innovations and post-processing refinements. This paper introduces a data-centric optimization by proposing a novel, physically grounded rotation representation through principal axes alignment. Our method aligns the object's coordinate system with its inherent geometric axes, derived from inertial properties, yielding three key advantages: Inherent Stability-leveraging the energy-minimizing property of principal axes provides a robust representation that is less sensitive to noise and occlusions; Symmetry-Aware Canonicalization-explicitly resolving rotational ambiguities for symmetric objects at the data level, which fundamentally eliminates label confusion during network training; and Framework Agnosticism-the optimization is applied purely at the dataset level, ensuring plug-and-play compatibility with existing networks without any architectural modification. We validate the framework across diverse category-level and instance-level models. Extensive experiments demonstrate consistent and significant accuracy improvements, while preserving the integrity of the baseline network. This work establishes a new, geometry-driven direction for enhancing pose estimation, circumventing the need for complex network redesign.

</details>


### [52] [FIDA: Feature Instability-Driven Attack on Self-Supervised Facial Representation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26861)
*Zhiyang Chen,Changchun Yin,Huiqin Yang,Liming Fang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Introduces FIDA, a backdoor attack against self-supervised face representations using Feature Instability Loss to induce trigger sensitivity, enabling high attack success while preserving clean accuracy and evading defenses.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: SSL face representations entangle identity features; backdoor risk is systemic but understudied. Existing perturbation-based defenses rely on rigid feature patterns that may be evaded by instability-based attacks; there is a need to assess risks and improve defenses for SSL-based facial analysis.

Method: FIDA framework injects subtle semantic triggers and uses Feature Instability Loss to train the encoder to increase sensitivity of triggered features along perturbation directions sampled during attack optimization, preventing rigid backdoor patterns and enhancing stealth.

Result: High attack success rate with benign utility largely preserved across evaluated settings; robust against perturbation-based defenses; demonstrates a significant threat to real-world multimedia applications relying on facial analysis.

Conclusion: FIDA reveals a new class of backdoor attacks exploiting feature instability in self-supervised face representations, underscoring the need for defenses that account for instability and identity entanglement, and prompting further research into robust SSL defenses and backdoor detection.

Abstract: Self-supervised learning (SSL) models are vulnerable to backdoor attacks. However, the systemic risks they pose in face representation have received little attention. The entanglement of identity features in self-supervised face learning presents unique challenges for attack stealthiness. To address this gap, we propose FIDA (Feature Instability-Driven Attack), a novel backdoor attack framework. FIDA uses subtle semantic triggers for injection, but its key innovation is a novel objective called Feature Instability Loss. It trains the encoder to increase the sensitivity of triggered features along perturbation directions sampled during attack optimization . By preventing the backdoor from exhibiting the rigid feature patterns typical of previous attacks, FIDA effectively evades the evaluated perturbation-based defenses. Experiments show that FIDA achieves a high attack success rate and generally preserves benign utility across the evaluated settings , posing a significant threat to real-world multimedia applications relying on facial analysis.

</details>


### [53] [Order Matters: A Chinese Multi-Panel Meme Benchmark for Vision-Language Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26866)
*Haihan Li,Haihao Li,Zhenfei Xu,Jize Qian*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: CMPM introduces a Chinese multi-panel meme benchmark (1,214 samples) to study order-aware multimodal reasoning; LVLMs struggle with panel ordering even when canonical displays perform well; Gemini 3.1 Pro and GPT-5.5 lead the task2 explanations; dataset and code to be released.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address the gap in understanding LVLMs' ability to perform sequence-aware reasoning on structured memes, especially in Chinese social media, where punchlines rely on panel order and cross-modal cues.

Method: Construct CMPM with five structural types and ordering constraints; two tasks: Task1 structure typing and order-sensitive panel sequencing (with context ablation), Task2 Chinese meme explanation generation with five human-rated dimensions; evaluate five representative LVLMs under a unified protocol.

Result: Canonical-display accuracy alone is insufficient; shuffling panels causes a sharp accuracy drop, indicating a gap in order-sensitive multimodal reasoning. In Task2, Gemini 3.1 Pro and GPT-5.5 outperform open models; context provides only a small, mixed gain.

Conclusion: There is a persistent need for order-aware multimodal reasoning in memes; CMPM provides a benchmark to study this, with expectations that released code/data will spur development of better sequence-aware models.

Abstract: Many multimodal tasks depend on how visual elements are ordered and composed, not only on recognizing them in isolation. Internet memes are a compact case of this problem: their punchline often depends on a constrained reading order and cross-panel visual--textual cues. While large vision-language models (LVLMs) show strong performance on single-image understanding, it remains unclear whether they can perform sequence-aware reasoning over structured meme layouts, especially in Chinese social media. We introduce CMPM, a Chinese Multi-Panel Meme benchmark with 1,214 annotated samples covering five structural types, ordering dependency, panel-order constraints, and optional comment context. We formulate a two-layer evaluation: Task1 probes structure typing and order-sensitive panel sequencing (with a context ablation setting), and Task2 evaluates Chinese meme explanation generation with human ratings on five 1-3 Likert dimensions (visual, panel, humor, context, and faithfulness). We benchmark five representative LVLMs under a unified protocol. Results indicate that canonical-display accuracy is not by itself evidence of order understanding: the primary shuffled condition produces a sharp accuracy drop, revealing a persistent gap in order-sensitive multimodal reasoning. Task2 preferences place Gemini 3.1 Pro and GPT-5.5 above the open models, while comment context yields only a small and mixed Core4 gain. Code and data will be released upon acceptance.

</details>


### [54] [Self-OPD: On-Policy Distillation for Flow Matching Models without Teacher](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26872)
*Shiyi Zhang,Mushui Liu,Yunze Tong,Wanggui He,Siyu Zou,Jinlong Liu,Yunlong Yu,Jian Song,Hao Jiang,Pipei Huang,Bo Zheng*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Teacher-free on-policy distillation for flow models that uses self-exploration: at each step, generate K stochastic SDE candidates from the deterministic next state, compare rewards to a self reference, and train via an all-branch pull-push objective with variance-aware normalization; fused multi-objective rewards.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Reduce computational cost and distribution mismatch of OPD by removing task-specific teachers and leveraging the student’s own exploration to provide supervision, while addressing multi-objective alignment.

Method: At each timestep, branch the deterministic next-state prediction into K stochastic SDE candidates, roll out with an ODE sampler, and compute normalized advantages against a deterministic self-reference baseline. Optimize velocity field with an all-branch pull-push objective: high-advantage branches attract, low-advantage branches repel, using direction-aware attenuation and SDE-variance normalization. For multi-objective alignment, fuse normalized reward scores at the reward level rather than via gradient conflicts.

Result: Self-OPD outperforms prior RL and OPD methods on single and mixed reward benchmarks without task-specific teachers.

Conclusion: Self-OPD provides a practical, teacher-free OPD framework for flow matching that mitigates teacher-student distribution gaps and supports multi-objective alignment, with strong empirical gains.

Abstract: On-policy distillation (OPD), which leverages a pre-trained, specialized teacher model to provide dense supervisory signals, has achieved significant success in Large Language Models (LLMs) and has recently been adapted to flow matching models. However, this paradigm suffers from two major issues: First, training a separate, task-specific teacher for every new objective incurs high computational costs. Second, the discrepancy between teacher and student distributions often leads to compounding errors along the generation trajectory. In this paper, we introduce \textbf{Self-OPD}, a teacher-free OPD framework for flow matching models that turns the student's own self-exploration into step-wise supervision. At each timestep, Self-OPD branches the deterministic next-state prediction into $K$ stochastic SDE candidates, rolls them out with the ODE sampler, and compares their rewards against a deterministic self-reference baseline to obtain normalized advantages. The velocity field is optimized with an all-branch pull-push objective, where high-advantage branches attract the student and low-advantage branches repel it under direction-aware attenuation and SDE-variance normalization. For multi-objective alignment, Self-OPD fuses normalized scores at the reward level, avoiding direct gradient conflict. Experiments on single and mixed reward benchmarks show that Self-OPD outperforms prior RL and OPD methods without task-specific teachers.

</details>


### [55] [Tether the Subject, Release the Scene: Query-Aware Memory Routing for Long-Horizon Autoregressive Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26902)
*Chen Li,Peng Zhang,Hanyu Zhou,Jialong Zuo,Fei Wang,Daiguo Zhou,Nong Sang,Changxin Gao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TetherMem: a training-free, query-aware spatiotemporal memory router that decouples subject and scene queries to mitigate memory-anchored scene under-progression in streaming video generation; improves overall quality and scene progression without training, validated on pairwise judgments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address memory-anchored scene under-progression in streaming autoregressive video models, where subject-centric memory stabilizes identity but locks backgrounds and scene structure to past states, degrading scene dynamics.

Method: Introduce TetherMem, a memory router that is training-free, separates subject and scene queries, and applies region- and age-conditioned priors to modulate historical access; subject queries preserve identity-bearing history while scene queries reduce reliance on subject history to allow background and scene changes.

Result: Outperforms eight baselines on 2,400 blinded pairwise judgments (overall quality 0.780, scene progression 0.769). On 30-second videos, maintains changes in background, viewpoint, and scene state while preserving subject recognizability and temporal continuity.

Conclusion: Training-free, query-aware spatiotemporal memory routing via subject/scene query separation and priors effectively mitigates memory-anchored under-progression, enabling more natural long-video generation without sacrificing identity or continuity.

Abstract: Streaming autoregressive video models generate long videos chunk by chunk, using historical memory to maintain consistency. Existing methods typically expose subject and scene queries to history through similar policies. This stabilizes the subject, but can also lock backgrounds, viewpoints, and scene structure to previously generated states even when local motion continues. We call this failure memory-anchored scene under-progression; consistency and motion metrics alone can miss it. We introduce TetherMem, a training-free, query-aware spatiotemporal memory router for frozen video generators. TetherMem separates subject and scene queries and modulates historical access with region- and age-conditioned priors: subject queries retain identity-bearing history, while scene queries reduce reliance on subject history and stale backgrounds. Across 2,400 blinded pairwise judgments from 10 annotators, TetherMem achieves the highest estimated expected preference among eight streaming long-video baselines for overall quality (0.780) and scene progression (0.769). On complete 30-second videos, it sustains changes in background, viewpoint, and scene state while preserving subject recognizability and temporal continuity.

</details>


### [56] [AraMS-28k: The Largest Publicly Released Line-Level Dataset of Historical Arabic Manuscripts with Margin and Insertion-Anchor Annotations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26921)
*Mohamed Guechaoui,Mohamed Diaa Zellagui,Souleyman Chaib,Sahraoui Dhelim*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: AraMS-28k: a large, publicly released line-level dataset of historical Arabic manuscripts (14 books, 3,043 pages, 28,600 lines) with main-text and margin annotations, insertion anchors for reading order, and dual transcripts (diacritised and diacritic-normalised).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address data scarcity for historical Arabic manuscript OCR and reading-order recovery; enable robust cross-script HTR, layout analysis, and non-linear reading-order research; provide a reference-grounded, peer-reviewed corpus with quality control.

Method: Collect 13 hand-copied manuscripts across Naskh, Ruq'ah, Maghrebi and one lithographed edition; annotate at line level for main-text vs margin; annotate insertion anchors to recover non-linear reading order; provide both vocalised and normalised transcriptions; use RefLAM pipeline combining automated OCR alignment with human review; release page images, line annotations, and fixed train/val/test splits.

Result: Dataset comprises 3,043 pages and 28,600 lines (27,971 main-text, 629 margin), including margin anchors; cross-script generalisation analyzed via baseline HTR on Kraken and HATFormer; first public release with line-level non-linear reading-order annotations for historical Arabic manuscripts; CC BY-NC-SA 4.0.

Conclusion: AraMS-28k enables reproducible research in Arabic manuscript recognition, layout analysis, and reading-order recovery; supports cross-script generalization studies and robust evaluation; provides a comprehensive benchmark with transparent QA via RefLAM.

Abstract: We introduce AraMS-28k, the largest publicly released line-level dataset of genuine historical Arabic manuscripts, comprising 14 books, 3,043 pages, and 28,600 annotated text lines (27,971 main-text, 629 margin). Thirteen books are hand-copied manuscripts spanning three script traditions -- Naskh, Ruq'ah, and Maghrebi -- and one is a lithographed printed edition included to broaden format diversity. Each line is labelled as main-text or margin, and margin lines that have an unambiguous attachment point in the main text are further annotated with an insertion anchor, recovering the manuscript's true non-linear reading order at line-level granularity -- to our knowledge the first such annotation released for a historical Arabic manuscript corpus. Because reference transcriptions are fully vocalised while manuscript hands are typically undiacritised, we release both the raw diacritised transcription and a diacritic-normalised counterpart for every line. The dataset was constructed with RefLAM, a reference-grounded annotation pipeline that aligns multimodal-LLM OCR against independently sourced clean transcriptions and routes every line through human review, combining automatic verification with expert oversight. We describe the construction and quality-control process, present the annotation schema, report dataset statistics at both the corpus and per-book level, and provide baseline HTR results using Kraken and HATFormer, including a cross-script generalisation gradient from in-distribution pages to fully unseen books. AraMS-28k is released with page images, line-level annotations, and fixed train/val/test splits under CC BY-NC-SA 4.0 to support reproducible research on Arabic manuscript recognition, layout analysis, and reading-order recovery.

</details>


### [57] [KISS-GS: 3D Gaussian Splatting Compression Kept Simple](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26948)
*Wieland Morgenstern,Friedrich Elias Branschke,Florian Fleischmann,Adrian Szatmari,Paul Schlack,Florian Barthel,Peter Eisert,Anna Hilsmann*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: KISS-GS is a modular, training-decoupled compression pipeline for 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) that achieves 85×–319× reductions in scene size by combining pruning-based compaction, image-based encoding (SOG-XT) with Self-organizing 2D codebooks and PRAS, plus optional encoding-aware fine-tuning; decoding uses web-native image formats and the design supports component reuse.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 3DGS scenes produce massive uncompressed data; existing compression pipelines mix compression with training, obscuring where gains come from and hindering reuse. A simple, modular, training-agnostic pipeline is desired to improve transparency and mix-and-match components across training pipelines.

Method: 1) Prune the vanilla 3DGS to compact the scene by 15.7× using state-of-the-art pruning strategies. 2) Encode the pruned scene into an image-based format (SOG-XT) with two innovations: (a) Self-organizing 2D Codebooks and (b) Parallel Representative Assignment Smoothing (PRAS), leveraging quaternion and scale parameter symmetries to produce 2D attribute grids amenable to encoding. 3) Optional encoding-aware fine-tuning provides an additional 2.2× reduction. Decoding relies on standard web-native image formats, enabling easy deployment.

Result: Across standard 3DGS benchmarks, the pipeline achieves 85× to 319× reductions in scene size compared to uncompressed vanilla 3DGS, setting new benchmarks for real-world scenes and outperforming tightly integrated methods in rate-distortion.

Conclusion: A simple, modular compression framework that decouples compression from training can deliver substantial, transparent gains and broad compatibility for future reconstruction and compaction advances, with decoding relying on ubiquitous image formats and easy component reuse.

Abstract: Scene reconstruction with 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) has become common, however deployment remains painful as the uncompressed file sizes can be massive. Current 3DGS compression systems combine multiple strategies for file size reduction, which can obscure where gains come from and limit component reuse across training pipelines. To make the gains more transparent, we propose KISS-GS, a modular compression pipeline named after the principle of keeping things simple, designed to decouple compression entirely from training.
  Given a 3DGS scene reconstructed with vanilla 3DGS, we are able to reduce it through compaction by 15.7x using a combination of state-of-the-art pruning schemes. Then we encode it into an image-based format designed for simple, ubiquitous decoding. With the SOG-XT format, we propose a novel extension to Self-Organizing Gaussians with two main contributions: (i) Self-organizing 2D Codebooks and (ii) Parallel Representative Assignment Smoothing (PRAS), which leverages the symmetry of quaternion and scale parameterizations to produce 2D attribute grids more amenable to encoding.
  This encoding reduces scene size by 6.6x. We show that optional encoding-aware fine-tuning yields a further 2.2x. Across standard 3DGS benchmarks, our simple and modular approach thus achieves a total of 85x to 319x reductions in the size of the scene over uncompressed vanilla 3DGS, setting new benchmarks for real-world scenes and surpassing tightly integrated methods in rate-distortion. Decoding relies solely on web-native image formats, and the modular design makes each stage easy to combine with future advances in reconstruction and compaction.
  Code and project page: https://fraunhoferhhi.github.io/KISS-GS/

</details>


### [58] [Per-View Gaussian Predictions Enable Training-Free Distractor Filtering in Feed-Forward 3DGS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26951)
*Kangmin Seo,Jae-Pil Heo*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A training-free filtering method removes transient-object artifacts in feed-forward 3D Gaussian Splatting by excluding per-view Gaussians and verifying candidate inconsistencies via multi-view rendering, improving novel-view quality across models and datasets without retraining.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Transient objects appearing in only a subset of views can be embedded in per-view Gaussians, corrupting the final novel views. A lightweight, training-free filter aims to suppress such artifacts without scene-specific optimization.

Method: For each input view, temporarily remove its associated Gaussians and render the scene with the remaining representation. Identify candidate artifact regions via feature similarity between the original and inconsistent renderings. Verify candidates by checking whether their removal reduces reconstruction error in other input views. The procedure operates on a single frozen prediction and does not involve retraining or per-scene optimization.

Result: Across three reconstruction models and two distractor benchmarks, the method consistently improves novel-view quality across varying numbers of input views. On clean scenes, the original reconstructions are largely preserved, indicating limited disruption to artifact-free content.

Conclusion: A lightweight, training-free filter can effectively suppress transient-object artifacts in feed-forward Gaussian Splatting without retraining, improving robustness across models and datasets while preserving fidelity on clean scenes.

Abstract: Feed-forward 3D Gaussian Splatting reconstructs an explicit Gaussian representation from multiple input images in one network execution, making 3D reconstruction increasingly accessible for casual captures. However, such captures frequently contain transient objects that appear in only a subset of the views. Such content can be encoded into the per-view Gaussians associated with the inputs that observe it and remain in the combined representation despite being observed by no other input. As a result, it may produce blurred, duplicated, or floating artifacts in novel views. We introduce a training-free filtering procedure that exploits this per-view prediction structure. For each input, we exclude its associated Gaussians and render the same camera using the remaining representation, revealing content that is inconsistent with the other inputs. Feature similarity forms candidate regions, and rendering-based verification retains only candidates whose removal reduces reconstruction error in the other input views. The procedure operates on a single frozen prediction without retraining or scene-specific optimization. Across three reconstruction models and two distractor benchmarks, it consistently improves novel-view quality with varying numbers of input views. On clean scenes, evaluations across four models show that the original reconstructions are largely preserved.

</details>


### [59] [RubricRM: Generative Reward Modeling via Dynamic Rubrics for Image Generation and Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26956)
*Zijian Kan,Wei Wang,Long Luo,Bing Zhao,Xuan Ren,Weixu Qiao,Wenbo Li,Hu Wei,Lin Qu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: RubricRM introduces a context-aware, rubric-based reward model for text-to-image generation and image editing, enabling input-specific evaluation beyond single scalar scores and fixed criteria.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing visual reward models rely on a single score or fixed criteria, limiting interpretability and task sensitivity across diverse inputs and instructions in text-to-image generation and editing.

Method: Train dedicated RubricRM systems using a two-stage pipeline: (1) supervised fine-tuning to learn rubric-based scoring, and (2) GRPO-based fine-grained dimension-level rewards to further boost scoring. Each RubricRM outputs an input-specific rubric (dimensions, weights, and criteria) and then scores candidate images against it.

Result: RubricRM outperforms existing specialized reward models and remains competitive with large proprietary LLM-based judges on multiple generation and editing benchmarks, despite using smaller backbones. Data and code are publicly available.

Conclusion: A flexible, interpretable reward modeling framework that adapts to different instructions and tasks, improving evaluation quality for text-to-image generation and image editing while maintaining efficiency.

Abstract: Reward models play an essential role in aligning visual generative models, yet most existing visual reward models use a single scalar score or rely on fixed criteria that cannot adapt to different instructions. This limits both interpretability and task sensitivity, especially for text-to-image generation and instruction-based image editing, where different inputs require different evaluation dimensions. We propose RubricRM, a pairwise generative reward modeling framework that first produces an input-specific rubric with evaluation dimensions, weights, and scoring criteria, and then applies the rubric to score candidate images. We train dedicated RubricRM models for text-to-image generation and image editing using a two-stage training pipeline: supervised fine-tuning teaches the model the rubric-based scoring paradigm, while GRPO further improves scoring through fine-grained dimension-level rewards. Experiments on multiple generation and editing benchmarks show that RubricRM outperforms existing specialized reward models and remains competitive with strong proprietary MLLM judges despite using smaller backbones. Our models, data, and code are available at https://github.com/zijiankan/RubricRM.

</details>


### [60] [Geo-LoRA: Geometry-Aware Subspace Evolution for Low-Rank Adaptation in Continual Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26960)
*Yibo Feng*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Geo-LoRA introduces a geometry-aware framework for rehearsal-free CIL with LoRA adapters, regulating the evolution of shared and task-specific low-rank subspaces to improve stability and plasticity, and achieving state-of-the-art results without extra adapter types.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Rehearsal-free CIL with LoRA can suffer from drift and collapse of subspaces across tasks because low-rank updates evolve without geometric control, leading to unstable shared representations and repetitive interference between tasks.

Method: For the shared branch: Subspace Projection Preservation (SPP) constrains consecutive updates to follow smooth trajectories on the Grassmann manifold; Adaptive Core-Slack Alignment (ACSA) decomposes transitions into principal and residual components, aligning the principal part while modulating the residual to balance stability and plasticity. For the task-specific branch: Median-Calibrated Block Overlap (MCBO) imposes a statistical constraint via normalized projection overlap to penalize excessive reuse. These constraints jointly regulate the evolution of all LoRA subspaces across layers and tasks without introducing additional adapter types beyond standard LoRA.

Result: Geo-LoRA achieves state-of-the-art performance on multiple continual learning benchmarks across varying task lengths, demonstrating robust stabilization of subspace evolution and reduced subspace crowding.

Conclusion: The work provides a principled geometric formulation for continual low-rank adaptation in rehearsal-free CIL with LoRA, enabling improved stability-plasticity balance and consistent performance gains without expanding the adapter taxonomy.

Abstract: Rehearsal-free class-incremental learning (CIL) with LoRA adapters remains challenging because the low-rank subspaces updated across tasks evolve without geometric control, causing unstable shared representations and repetitive collapse of task-specific updates into previously occupied directions. We introduce Geo-LoRA, a geometry-aware framework that explicitly regulates how low-rank subspaces, both shared and task-specific, evolve during continual learning. For the shared branch, Subspace Projection Preservation (SPP) constrains consecutive updates to follow smooth trajectories on the Grassmann manifold, and Adaptive Core-Slack Alignment (ACSA) decomposes transitions into principal and residual components, aligning the former while modulating the latter to balance stability and plasticity. For the task-specific branch, Median-Calibrated Block Overlap (MCBO) imposes a statistical constraint via normalized projection overlap, penalizing excessive reuse to mitigate subspace crowding. These constraints jointly regulate the evolution of all LoRA subspaces across layers and tasks without introducing additional adapter types beyond standard LoRA. Geo-LoRA provides a principled geometric formulation for continual low-rank adaptation and consistently achieves state-of-the-art performance across multiple benchmark datasets and different task lengths.

</details>


### [61] [TempJail: Temporal Jailbreak Attacks against Image-to-Video Generation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26971)
*Qi Lu,Zehui Guo,David Yuanda Gan,Zijing Li,Hengda Zhang,Weijun Xu,Qiankun Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TempJail reveals a temporal vulnerability in image-to-video (I2V) models: unsafe semantics can emerge over time due to semantic composition across frames. It introduces temporal abstraction (initial visual condition + temporal instruction) and semantic camouflage (latent perturbations in diffusion sampling guided by encoders; innocuous text template) to trigger malicious semantics in black-box inference, outperforming prior methods on multiple closed-source models. Code released.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current safety studies for I2V focus on single-frame jailbreaks; temporal dynamics in video generation can accumulate unsafe semantics, creating a gap in defenses. TempJail investigates how malicious semantics can stealthily unfold over time.

Method: Propose TempJail with two-pronged approach: (1) temporal abstraction by splitting target malicious caption into an initial frame visual condition and a temporal text instruction; (2) semantic camouflage by (a) injecting semantics via controlled latent perturbations during diffusion sampling with gradient guidance from pretrained encoders, and (b) rewriting the caption into an innocuous "subject-action-scene" template that preserves temporal guidance while bypassing filters. Attacks are executed in a black-box I2V setting where both modalities cooperatively trigger harmful semantics over time.

Result: Empirical evaluation on closed-source models (Kling, Seedance, Veo, PixVerse) shows TempJail improves attack success rate by 23.3% under GPT-5.2 evaluation and 22.0% under human evaluation compared to prior state-of-the-art methods. Code available at GitHub.

Conclusion: Highlights a new class of temporally cumulative safety vulnerabilities in I2V systems. Demonstrates the need for defenses addressing temporal semantics, robust safety filtering for video streams, and resilience against joint multimodal (image+text) jailbreaks.

Abstract: In recent years, image-to-video (I2V) generation models have made remarkable progress in subject consistency and temporal coherence, enabling high quality video synthesis. However, these advances also introduce new safety risks. Existing studies mainly focus on jailbreak attacks involving single frame violations, while largely overlooking the temporal dimension unique to video generation models. In this paper, we investigate three attack scenarios and uncover a temporal vulnerability in I2V systems: unsafe semantics may emerge not from a single frame, but from semantic composition over time. We further identify two key challenges in such attacks: temporal abstraction and semantic camouflage. To address these issues, we propose TempJail, a novel temporal jailbreak framework for I2V systems. For temporal abstraction, we decompose a target malicious caption into an initial frame visual condition and a temporal text instruction. For semantic camouflage, on the image side we model semantic injection as controlled latent perturbation in diffusion sampling and introduce gradient guidance from pretrained encoders. On the text side, we rewrite the caption into an innocuous ``subject-action-scene'' template that bypasses safety filters while preserving temporal guidance. In the black-box inference phase, these two modalities jointly enable malicious semantics to be gradually triggered over time. Experiments on closed-source commercial models, including Kling, Seedance, Veo and PixVerse, show that TempJail improves attack success rate over prior state-of-the-art methods by 23.3\% under GPT-5.2 evaluation and 22.0\% under human evaluation. Our codes are available at \href{https://github.com/luqi-glory/TempJail}{GitHub}.

</details>


### [62] [Aphanta: Diagnosing Task-Aligned Image-Edited Intermediates for Multimodal Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26993)
*Hengyuan Xu,Wei Cheng,Yumeng Ji,Xuanyang Zhang,Xianfang Zeng,Gang Yu,Xingjun Ma*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Aphanta introduces a closed-loop evaluation framework for MLLM→image editor→MLLM pipelines to separate practical editor utility from intrinsic reasoning. Across 20 tasks, editors show task-conditioned gains with improvements mainly in visual cueing, grounding, and counterfactual state realization; symbol-sensitive or structurally extrapolative tasks remain unreliable. On positive tasks, mean score rises from 0.343 to 0.445 using the Qwen pipeline (+10.2 points; +29.7% relative). Aphanta positions image editing as a specialized visual workspace and provides a reusable protocol for assessing task–representation alignment and downstream utility.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To disentangle an MLLM's pure reasoning ability from its ability to leverage an external image editor, and to quantify how well editors realize the required transformations across diverse tasks.

Method: Three evaluation conditions are tested: direct MLLM reasoning, reasoning with an editor-generated intermediate, and reasoning with an idealized reference intermediate. The study covers 20 candidate tasks, multiple editor–MLLM combinations, and a positive-task subset, with results reported via a consolidated Qwen pipeline.

Result: Findings show strong task conditioning of utility; gains concentrate in visual cue injection, grounding, and counterfactual state realization, while symbol-sensitive construction and structural extrapolation perform poorly. The full study preserves filtered/unsuccessful tasks to reveal boundaries; mean task score on positive tasks improves from 0.343 to 0.445.

Conclusion: Image editing functions as a specialized visual workspace rather than a universal reasoning mechanism. Aphanta provides a reusable protocol for measuring task–representation alignment, editor realization, and downstream pipeline utility.

Abstract: Explicit visual intermediates can help multimodal large language models (MLLMs) externalize spatial evidence and updated visual states, but their utility depends on whether an image editor can faithfully realize the required transformation. We introduce \textbf{Aphanta}, an automated task-discovery and closed-loop diagnostic framework for the MLLM -> image editor -> MLLM pipeline. Aphanta evaluates three conditions---direct reasoning, reasoning with an editor-generated intermediate, and reasoning with an idealized reference intermediate---to separate potential visual headroom from the practical utility of current editors. Across 20 candidate tasks and multiple editor--MLLM combinations, we find that utility is strongly task-conditioned. Gains concentrate in visual cue injection, grounding, and counterfactual state realization, whereas intermediates requiring symbol-sensitive construction or structural extrapolation are substantially less reliable. On the selected positive-task subset, our consolidated Qwen pipeline improves the mean task score from 0.343 to 0.445 ($+10.2$ points; $+29.7\%$ relative), while the full study also retains filtered and unsuccessful tasks to expose the boundary. These results position image editing as a specialized visual workspace rather than a universal reasoning mechanism, and establish Aphanta as a reusable protocol for measuring task--representation alignment, editor realization, and downstream pipeline utility.

</details>


### [63] [Virtual iEEG from Scalp EEG: Charting the Landscape of Source Imaging, Intracranial Inference and Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26998)
*Dongyi He,Xiangkai Wang,Hongjie Yan,Luping Song,Wai Ting Siok,Nizhuan Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Virtual iEEG enables noninvasive inference of some intracranial events and representations from scalp data, but cannot fully recover arbitrary contact-level activity; validation needs stronger controls and prospective datasets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome iEEG invasiveness and limited spatial coverage by enabling scalp-based inference of intracranial signals.

Method: A target-centered framework distinguishing event inference, feature translation, and waveform reconstruction; separates predictability, observability, identifiability, fidelity, utility; evaluates evidence by cohort independence, anatomical/spectral coverage, train-test separation, and target adaptation.

Result: Current studies show inference of selected intracranial events, low-frequency components, and task-related representations; not unique recovery of arbitrary contact-level activity. Validation requires controls, baselines, uncertainty assessment, and incremental-utility testing.

Conclusion: Advancement depends on independent paired datasets and prospective demonstration that virtual iEEG adds value beyond scalp EEG and EEG source imaging.

Abstract: Intracranial electroencephalography (iEEG) provides temporally precise and spatially specific access to neural activity from focal and deep brain regions, but its invasiveness and restricted anatomical coverage limit routine use. These constraints have motivated scalp-to-intracranial inference, termed virtual iEEG when model outputs carry iEEG-defined event, feature, representation, or contact-level waveform semantics. This review presents a target-centred framework distinguishing event inference, feature translation, and waveform reconstruction, while separating predictability from observability, identifiability, fidelity, and utility. Evidence is evaluated according to cohort independence, anatomical and spectral coverage, train--test separation, and target-patient adaptation. Current studies support inference of selected intracranial events, low-frequency components, and task-related representations, but not unique recovery of arbitrary contact-level activity. Stronger validation requires appropriate controls, source-imaging baselines, uncertainty assessment, and incremental-utility testing. Future progress depends on independent paired datasets and prospective evidence that virtual iEEG adds value beyond scalp EEG and EEG source imaging.

</details>


### [64] [MVC-Bench: Benchmarking Calibration of Medical Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27004)
*Ashshak Sharifdeen,Shihab Aaqil Ahamed,Ufaq Khan,Muhammad Akhtar Munir Sujair Ibrahim,Mohamed Rafeek Mareer Ahamed,Yutong Xie,Imran Razzak,Muhammad Haris Khan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MVC-Bench is a calibration-centric benchmark for medical VLMs, assessing and improving calibration (ECE, MCE, ACE) across modalities, backbones, and domain shifts, and proposing a train-time calibration method (MCM) that improves calibration while maintaining accuracy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Calibration of vision-language models in medical settings is underexplored; reliable confidence estimates are crucial for clinical safety under realistic conditions.

Method: Comprehensive benchmark with eight backbones, three medical modalities (fundus, histopathology, chest X-ray); in-domain and domain-shift settings; compares post-hoc, training-time calibration, and zero-shot methods; evaluates six prompt-tuning methods; reports accuracy, ECE, MCE, ACE across 1,638 controlled experiments; analyzes miscalibration causes; introduces Multi-Class Margin (MCM) regularization for train-time calibration.

Result: MCM achieves the lowest ECE in 10 of 12 in-domain settings and remains competitive under domain shifts; MVC-Bench delivers a structured evaluation framework and actionable guidance for improving calibration in safety-critical medical workflows.

Conclusion: MVC-Bench provides a calibration-focused evaluation framework, enabling robust assessment and improvement of VLMs and Medical-VLMs in medical workflows, with practical recommendations for calibrating models under modality, backbone, and domain shift variations.

Abstract: Reliable evaluation of vision-language models (VLMs) and medical vision-language models (Medical-VLMs) requires calibrated confidence, particularly under realistic clinical conditions. However, existing efforts mainly focused on improving accuracy, leaving calibration in the medical domain underexplored. To this end, we propose MVC-Bench, a calibration-centric benchmark for medical image classification with VLMs and Medical-VLMs. MVC-Bench assesses the calibration across three axes: (i) robustness to modality, backbone, and domain shift (ii) effectiveness of calibration strategies and prompt-tuning methods (iii) stability under prompt-template and random-seed variations. The benchmark covers eight different backbones, three medical modalities, including fundus imaging, histopathology, and chest X-ray under in-domain and domain shift settings. It compares post-hoc calibration, train-time calibration, and zero-shot inference methods, together with six prompt-tuning methods. Across more than 1638 controlled experiments, we report accuracy and Expected Calibration Error (ECE) as primary metrics, and further report results with complementary calibration measures, including Maximum Calibration Error (MCE) and Adaptive Calibration Error (ACE). We further investigate the underlying causes of miscalibration in VLMs and Medical-VLMs and propose a simple train-time calibration method, Multi-Class Margin (MCM) regularization, which achieves lowest ECE on 10 out of 12 settings in in-domain and remains competitive under domain shifts. Collectively, MVC-Bench provides a structured evaluation framework and actionable guidance for improving calibration in safety-critical medical workflows.

</details>


### [65] [Differentiable Jitter Correction using Deep Learning-based Image Quality Metric for Phase-Contrast Micro-CT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27034)
*Junan Chen,Yiting Jia,Joscha Maier,Dominik John,Sami Wirtensohn,Imke Greving,Silja Flenner,Matthias Wieczorek,Julia Herzen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A differentiable jitter-correction framework for X-ray phase-contrast micro-CT that uses a deep-learning image quality proxy (VIF) to estimate and compensate per-projection jitter without a reference, combining a gradient-based auto-focus in parallel-beam geometry, a 3D-CNN predictor of VIF from corrupted volumes, and a selective background TV penalty; validated on synchrotron data showing recovery of fine structural detail and cross-sample generalization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Phase-contrast micro-CT suffers from projection jitter that degrades high-frequency details. Existing solutions often require a motion-free reference; there is a need for a fully differentiable, reference-free correction method that can generalize across samples and beamlines.

Method: A gradient-based auto-focus optimization in parallel-beam geometry to estimate per-projection jitter; evaluation of candidate objective functions for jitter sensitivity; train a compact 3D CNN to predict the VIF image-quality metric from a single corrupted volume; apply a spatially selective total-variation penalty to the image background to suppress spurious high-frequency artifacts during optimization; validate on biological specimens at multiple synchrotron beamlines with jitter applied to both simulated and real projection data.

Result: The integrated pipeline reliably recovers fine structural details lost to jitter and demonstrates generalization across morphologically distinct samples, with performance consistent across simulated and experimentally acquired data under varying beamline conditions.

Conclusion: A fully differentiable, reference-free jitter-correction framework for X-ray phase-contrast micro-CT that leverages a learned image-quality proxy (VIF) and a background TV prior, achieving robust restoration of fine features and cross-sample generalization; applicable to other imaging contexts with similar motion artifacts.

Abstract: This paper proposes a fully differentiable jitter correction method for X-ray phase-contrast micro computed tomography using a deep learning-based image quality metric that estimates and compensates per-projection rigid jitter directly from the acquired projection data, without a pre-scan motion-free reference. The approach builds on a gradient-based auto-focus strategy adapted to parallel-beam geometry. A set of candidate objective functions is benchmarked in a controlled study, and the sensitivity of the visual information fidelity (VIF) metric to the jitter artifact is verified with the target phase-contrast data. To operate without a clean reference, a compact 3D convolutional neural network is trained to predict the VIF score from a single corrupted volume. A spatially selective total variation penalty applied exclusively to the image background is introduced to penalize spurious high-frequency structures that otherwise emerge during optimization. Experiments on biological specimens acquired at different synchrotron beamlines are conducted. Evaluation uses jitter motion applied to simulated and experimentally acquired projection data. The result confirms that the integrated pipeline reliably recovers fine structural detail lost due to jitter, with generalization demonstrated across morphologically distinct samples.

</details>


### [66] [Multi-Person Human Motion Forecasting in Complex Scenes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27039)
*Serdar Ozsoy,Lars Doorenbos,Juergen Gall*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: OCSD is a diffusion-based model that unifies motion history, social interactions, and object cues for multi-person trajectory forecasting, achieving state-of-the-art results and multimodal future samples.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Accurately forecasting human movement in complex scenes requires integrating environment context (objects) and social interactions; prior methods struggle to fuse these components effectively.

Method: A conditional diffusion framework with an object-conditioning mechanism that modulates denoising at every timestep and a social encoder that models interactions among all humans; handles varying group sizes and enables sampling of multiple plausible futures.

Result: State-of-the-art on HiK and HOI-M3; reduces two-second path error by 121.5 mm (31.3%) on HiK and 130.5 mm (33.2%) on HOI-M3; yields more realistic long-term forecasts.

Conclusion: OCSD effectively fuses motion history, social dynamics, and object cues within a single diffusion framework, improving accuracy, realism, and multimodal forecasting in complex scenes.

Abstract: Accurately forecasting the movement of people in complex scenes requires reasoning over the past and present state of the entire environment. In this context, effectively incorporating object information and social interactions into a unified framework remains particularly challenging. To address this, we propose Object-Conditioned Social Diffusion (OCSD), a conditional diffusion model that integrates motion history, multi-person interactions, and object cues into a single framework. OCSD uses an object-conditioning mechanism that modulates denoising at every timestep, enabling fine-grained human-object reasoning, and a social encoder that models the interactions between all humans in the scene. As a result, our model naturally handles varying group sizes, complex social interactions, and supports sampling multiple plausible futures. Extensive experiments show that OCSD achieves state-of-the-art results on the Humans in Kitchens (HiK) and HOI-M3 benchmarks. It reduces the two-second path error by 121.5 mm (31.3%) on HiK and 130.5 mm (33.2%) on HOI-M3 compared to prior work, and produces more realistic long-term forecasts.

</details>


### [67] [Anatomy-Guided Foundation Model Adaptation with Within-Case Prototype Supervision for Standard Plane Detection in Fetal Ultrasound Blind Sweeps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27051)
*Yuzhe Zhao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: AnatoProto is a lightweight sequence-level framework that adapts a frozen BiomedCLIP encoder for fetal abdominal circumference standard-plane detection in low-cost blind sweeps, using anatomy-aware pooling, within-case prototype loss, cascade refinement, and a hybrid head to deliver state-of-the-art F1 on ACOUSLIC-AI; it also reveals non-additive interactions between components.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Tackle extreme frame-level class imbalance and short positive segments in fetal ultrasound sweeps; leverage frozen biomedical encoders with anatomy-aware aggregation and case-level supervision to improve detection of rare positive frames and coherent segments.

Method: Four components: (i) anatomy-weighted spatial pooling using nnU-Net abdominal-region probabilities to reweight BiomedCLIP patch tokens; (ii) within-case prototype loss aligning each frame embedding with the sweep-wide mean of positive frames; (iii) a three-stage cascade refinement (frame -> segment -> case-level rejecter) to enforce segment-level coherence; (iv) a hybrid prediction head modeling per-frame stability and inter-frame boundary transitions; evaluation on ACOUSLIC-AI.

Result: Achieves test F1 of 67.72 on ACOUSLIC-AI, +13.20 F1 over the strongest baseline (FetalCLIP + PRS) and +15.76 F1 over TriDet + PRS. A synergy analysis shows prototype loss and anatomy-weighted pooling interact non-additively: prototype loss alone lowers recall by 12 points, but with anatomy-weighted pooling recall increases by 6.5 points, indicating dependence on accurate within-case prototypes.

Conclusion: Demonstrates that combining anatomy-aware pooling with case-level prototypes and cascade refinement yields robust, lightweight detection of rare positive frames in sequential ultrasound data, and highlights non-additive component interactions requiring joint optimization.

Abstract: Detecting the fetal abdominal circumference standard plane in low-cost obstetric blind sweeps is a highly imbalanced frame-classification problem: positive frames account for under 3% of a sequence, form short contiguous segments, and are poorly handled by off-the-shelf ultrasound and vision foundation models. We propose AnatoProto, a lightweight sequence-level framework that adapts a frozen BiomedCLIP encoder to fetal blind sweeps through four components: (i) anatomy-weighted spatial pooling that uses nnU-Net abdominal-region probabilities as a spatial prior to reweight BiomedCLIP patch tokens, so frozen semantic features are aggregated onto anatomically meaningful regions; (ii) a within-case prototype loss that pulls each frame embedding toward the mean of positive frames of the same sweep, exploiting case-level structure unavailable at the frame level; (iii) a three-stage cascade refinement (frame->segment->case-level rejecter) that lifts the prediction unit from noisy frames to structurally-constrained segments; and (iv) a hybrid prediction head that jointly models per-frame stability and inter-frame boundary transitions to suppress boundary false positives. On the ACOUSLIC-AI benchmark, AnatoProto reaches a test F1 of 67.72, outperforming the strongest foundation-model baseline (FetalCLIP + PRS, F1 = 54.52) by +13.20 F1 and the strongest video temporal-action-detection baseline (TriDet + PRS) by +15.76 F1. A synergy study, backed by embedding geometry and paired-bootstrap confidence intervals, shows that the prototype loss and anatomy-weighted pooling are not additive: applied alone the prototype loss reduces recall by 12 points, but combined with anatomy-weighted pooling it increases recall by 6.5 points -- a sign-flip we trace to the accuracy of the within-case prototype.

</details>


### [68] [Video-OPSD: Exploiting Privileged Visual Evidence for On-Policy Self-Distillation in Video Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27065)
*Ziyue Wang,Shiqi Huang,Weiwen Xu,Bihan Wen,Xudong Jiang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Video-OPSD introduces evidence-grounded self-teacher and evidence-guided token optimization for on-policy self-distillation in Video-LLMs. It leverages privileged evidence frames within the video, improving distillation efficiency and performance with reduced training time, and matches GRPO performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: OPSD in Video-LLMs remains underexplored; video data contains temporal redundancy where only a subset of frames carries evidence. Exploiting this privileged information can improve learning efficiency and accuracy.

Method: 1) Evidence-Grounded Self-Teacher: train the teacher using only annotated evidence frames while the student reasons on the full video. 2) Evidence-Guided Token Optimization: assign adaptive weights to distillation tokens based on their reliance on evidence frames, emphasizing perceptually grounded reasoning.

Result: Consistent improvements over Standard OPSD across multiple backbones on video understanding and reasoning benchmarks; achieves results comparable to GRPO with substantially less training time.

Conclusion: Demonstrates an effective and efficient post-training paradigm for Video-LLMs by exploiting privileged visual evidence; Video-OPSD narrows the gap to GRPO with lower computational cost.

Abstract: On-policy self-distillation (OPSD) has recently emerged as an effective post-training paradigm that improves policy optimization through dense token-level supervision from a privileged self-teacher. Despite its promise, OPSD remains largely underexplored for Video Large Language Models (Video-LLMs). Existing methods typically construct privileged teachers by augmenting their context with additional information while keeping the primary input unchanged for both teacher and student. Video reasoning, however, offers a distinct source of privileged supervision within the primary input itself: long videos contain substantial temporal redundancy, and only a small subset of frames provides the evidence necessary to answer a question. Building on this observation, we present $\textbf{Video-OPSD}$, an OPSD framework that exploits privileged visual evidence for both self-teacher construction and knowledge transfer. First, our Evidence-Grounded Self-Teacher conditions the teacher exclusively on annotated evidence frames while the student continues to reason over the complete video. This focused visual input enables the teacher to provide more informative supervision. Second, our Evidence-Guided Token Optimization adaptively weights token-level distillation according to each reasoning token's reliance on privileged visual evidence, thereby emphasizing perceptually grounded reasoning. Experiments across video understanding and reasoning benchmarks show that $\textbf{Video-OPSD}$ consistently improves upon Standard OPSD across multiple backbones and achieves performance comparable to GRPO while requiring substantially less training time, establishing an effective and efficient post-training approach for Video-LLMs.

</details>


### [69] [Beyond Classification: Task-Dependent Learnability under Privacy-Motivated Image Transformations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27066)
*Leon Ranke,Wolfgang Hübner,Ronny Hug,Michael Arens,Jürgen Beyerer*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Compute-aware multi-task evaluation protocol for PETs in vision; shows classification alone misses cross-task effects; PETs with similar accuracy can differ in other tasks; advocates broader evaluation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Classification-based evaluation is insufficient as a proxy for general vision tasks; PETs may alter visual structure in ways that affect other tasks; need efficient proxy tasks to approximate downstream performance without retraining for every PET setting.

Method: Introduce a compute-aware multi-task protocol combining lightweight proxy tasks capturing complementary visual structure; evaluate across irreversible privacy transformations, key-based block primitives, and learnable image encryption; compare PETs on classification and other tasks.

Result: PETs with similar classification accuracy can diverge significantly on other tasks; the protocol reveals hidden trade-offs and underscores the inadequacy of classification-only reporting.

Conclusion: Recommend adoption of multi-task, compute-aware PET evaluation protocols to better characterize impact across diverse vision tasks and prevent overly optimistic assessments based on classification alone.

Abstract: Privacy-Enhancing Technologies (PETs) in computer vision often rely on noise or image perturbations to protect visual data while securely processing it, creating a trade-off between task performance and protection. This trade-off is commonly evaluated using image classification, which primarily captures semantic separability and remains robust despite significant geometric, spatial layout or local boundary alterations. As a result, it is too simplistic as a proxy for generic vision tasks. Exhaustive downstream-task evaluation, however, is computationally expensive because models must often be trained for each PET transformation and parameter setting. We therefore propose a compute-aware multi-task protocol for evaluating PETs in model training. It combines lightweight proxy tasks that target complementary aspects of visual structure while remaining simple and fast to compute. Across irreversible privacy transformations, key-based block primitives, and learnable image encryption schemes, we demonstrate that PETs with similar classification accuracy can differ substantially on other tasks. The outcomes highlight the need for PET evaluation protocols that move beyond classification-only reporting.

</details>


### [70] [Automated 2D and 3D Segmentation of AMD and DME Lesions in OCT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27095)
*Lucia Sundberg,Zhihao Zhao,M. Ali Nasseri*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Four lesion-segmentation pipelines (2D/3D AMD and DME) reach Dice 0.76–0.82 in-domain with strong calibration; ensemble improves robustness; on external OLIVES cohort, generalization shows biomarker-aligned signals but not yet clinically validated for automated lesion burden tracking.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address limited generalization of OCT lesion segmentation across different acquisition protocols; move beyond slice-level evaluation to full-volume, calibration-aware assessment.

Method: Develop four pipelines (2D/3D for AMD and DME); perform systematic ablations; implement full-volume calibration-aware evaluation; test generalization on OLIVES with proxy metrics (AUROC for biomarkers, CST correlation, longitudinal concordance); analyze ensemble composition as driver of improvement.

Result: In-domain Dice 0.76–0.82; volume and surface calibration metrics r_vol and r_surf ≥ 0.97; ensemble composition most consistent improvement; external validation shows predictions track biomarkers but weaker than in-domain, not a full validation of automated lesion-burden tracking.

Conclusion: A full-volume calibration-aware adoption framework and ensemble approach improves in-domain segmentation and provides a framework for generalization assessment; further work needed to establish clinical utility for automated lesion-burden tracking across diverse data.

Abstract: Age-related macular degeneration (AMD) and diabetic macular edema (DME) are leading causes of vision loss, and optical coherence tomography (OCT) is the standard modality for detecting and monitoring the subtle lesions that drive treatment decisions. Most deep-learning segmentation work for OCT is validated only in-domain, leaving generalization to clinical data collected under different acquisition protocols largely untested. This work develops and systematically ablates four lesion-segmentation pipelines -- 2D and 3D variants for AMD and DME -- reaching Dice scores of 0.76 to 0.82 with strong volumetric and surface calibration (r vol, r surf greater than or equal to 0.97 across all four pipelines) on an in-domain validation set. The ablation process establishes a full-volume, calibration-aware adoption standard that catches mechanisms an ordinary slice-level evaluation would keep, and identifies ensemble composition as the most consistent driver of improvement. To test generalization, the models are evaluated on OLIVES, an external clinical cohort with no lesion-level ground truth, using a proxy-metric framework built around biomarker AUROC, central subfield thickness (CST) correlation, and longitudinal concordance. Predictions track clinical biomarkers outside the training distribution, though less strongly than in-domain -- evidence for, not validation of, automated lesion-burden tracking as a clinical tool.

</details>


### [71] [EditaLive! Unified Character Video Editing for Live Streaming](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27123)
*Zhiyuan Li,Chi-Man Pun,Peng-Tao Jiang,Bo Li,Xiaodong Cun*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: EditaLive enables real-time, live-streamed, human-centric video editing by repurposing a pretrained image-animation model for instruction-based editing, and compressing it into a fast two-step sampler via aligned self-rollout distillation to ensure low latency and expression fidelity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Live streaming emphasizes the human subject and requires real-time, consistent facial expressions; existing video-editing methods are ill-suited due to latency and expression drift from offline, multi-step inferences.

Method: Start from a pretrained Wan-Animate image-animation model; adapt it for instruction-based editing using reference-frame editing and video reconstruction with the CharEdit-50K dataset; convert offline bidirectional generation to causal streaming; introduce aligned self-rollout distillation to create a two-step sampler with fixed RoPE and align forcing, and apply first-frame preserved sparse attention to reduce historical dependence and prevent appearance drift.

Result: Empirically achieves state-of-the-art editing quality with faithful facial-expression preservation and low-latency real-time streaming inference.

Conclusion: EditaLive provides a practical, high-fidelity, real-time framework for live-streamed character editing, addressing both appearance consistency and latency challenges through model reengineering and distillation-based compression.

Abstract: Conventional video editing primarily focuses on scene-level content, whereas live streaming places greater emphasis on the human subject. However, directly applying existing video-editing methods to human-centric live streaming remains challenging, as they may introduce facial-expression inconsistencies and typically depend on multiple offline inference steps, making them unsuitable for real-time interaction. We propose EditaLive, a novel framework for real-time streaming character video editing. In detail, we start from a pretrained image animation model (Wan-Animate), which naturally decouples appearance from motion, and repurpose it as the base model for instruction-based human-centric video editing by reference frame editing and video reconstruction via the collected CharEdit-50K dataset. Besides, we adapt the model from offline bidirectional to causal streaming generation, and design an aligned self-rollout distillation strategy that compresses the model into a two-step sampler, where fixed RoPE and align forcing reduce training--inference discrepancies, and first-frame preserved sparse attention filters redundant historical information to mitigate appearance drift. Extensive experiments demonstrate that EditaLive delivers state-of-the-art editing performance with faithful preservation of facial expressions and low-latency real-time streaming inference.

</details>


### [72] [ReViCo: Unveiling the Limitations of VLMs in Visual Text Understanding via Error Correction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27154)
*Bojun Zhang,Junhong Liang,Feifei Zhai,Fengxian Ji,Yu Zhou*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Introduces ReViCo, a benchmark for real-world visual text error correction to evaluate VLMs’ text understanding in images, comparing prompt-based and targeted training; reveals large gaps to human performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the limited ability of Vision-Language Models to understand and manipulate textual content in images, by providing a realistic benchmark focused on detecting and correcting text errors in visual contexts.

Method: Construct ReViCo from real-world images with embedded text, define a visual text error correction task, and evaluate VLMs under two paradigms: prompt-based prompting and targeted model training, plus analyses of error types and perception of text.

Result: Current state-of-the-art VLMs significantly underperform humans on the task; models frequently misperceive visual text and incur correction errors, indicating substantial room for improvement in text-aware visual understanding.

Conclusion: ReViCo offers a new, robust benchmark to drive development of VLMs with stronger visual-text understanding and correction capabilities, highlighting specific gaps and guiding future research on bridging vision and text comprehension.

Abstract: Vision Language Models (VLMs) have shown great success in general visual tasks, yet they still struggle to deeply understand text within images. In this paper, we introduce ReViCo (Real Visual Correction), a benchmark designed to evaluate VLM text understanding through a novel task of visual text error correction. ReViCo challenges models to identify and fix text errors in real-world images, which requires a profound understanding of the interplay between visual text and its surrounding visual context. We benchmark various VLMs using two distinct paradigms: prompt-based strategy and targeted model training, both aimed at pushing the limits of current models. Our experiments reveal a striking performance gap between even the best VLMs and human, and further analysis also shows that most models struggle to accurately perceive the visual text, resulting in frequent correction errors. By highlighting these gaps, ReViCo provides a new benchmark foundation for developing more robust and text-aware VLMs.

</details>


### [73] [Magpie: Real-Time World Renderer for Interactive Games](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27168)
*Xiaoyu Zhan,Xinyu Wang,Xiaohong Zhang,Huanjie Zhu,Tengjiao Sun,Pengcheng Fang,Jiaxing Yu,Yanwen Guo,Dongjie Fu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Magpie decouples gameplay from rendering to enable real-time, generative visuals in interactive games while preserving designability and reproducibility.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current pipelines for game visuals are resource-intensive and asset-heavy. While video foundation models show promise, interactive games require stable world states and repeatable interactions. A system-level approach is needed to apply generative models without sacrificing gameplay determinism.

Method: Architect a two-component pipeline: a Game Engine that executes gameplay, maintains world state, and produces white-box frames; a separate Render Server that consumes those frames and uses generative models to render the final visuals in real time, enabling on-the-fly visual generation without altering gameplay logic.

Result: The paper outlines a practical architecture and workflow that preserves gameplay designability and reproducibility while reducing reliance on fully authored visual assets. It demonstrates the feasibility of real-time generative rendering in games via a render-server at run time, aligned with white-box frame generation from the engine.

Conclusion: Magpie provides a viable system-level path for integrating generative models into real-time game rendering, balancing creative visual generation with deterministic gameplay and asset efficiency.

Abstract: Modern game development relies heavily on conventional graphics pipelines. High-quality visual content requires modeling, material authoring, animation, lighting, effects, and runtime optimization, making asset production expensive and extending the development cycle of game prototypes. Recently, video foundation models are beginning to change film and video production, but games differ from linear media, they require not only continuous and realistic imagery, but also stable and reproducible gameplay rules, object states, and interaction outcomes. We present Magpie, a real-time generative world-rendering system for interactive games. Magpie separates gameplay execution from visual generation. Designers define scenes and rules in a game engine. At runtime, the Game Engine resolves player actions and maintains world state, while an independent Render Server generates visual output from white-box frames produced by the engine. Magpie provides a system-level implementation path for applying generative models to real-time game rendering. It preserves gameplay designability and reproducibility, and reduces the dependence of early game prototypes on complete visual assets.

</details>


### [74] [Ancient-Bench: A Comprehensive Multi-millennial, Multi-medium, and Multi-script Benchmark for Ancient Chinese Artifact Text Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27169)
*Hiuyi Cheng,Nuo Xu,Yuyi Zhang,Xuhan Zheng,Wei Pan,Jing Zhang,Dezhi Peng,Minghui Liao,Yihua Teng,Jihao Wu,Haoyu Ren,Lianwen Jin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A new Ancient-Bench benchmark with 2,700 ancient Chinese artifact images across 3,000 years, 9 media types, and 7 scripts, plus symbol/character/parsing annotation standards; evaluation shows persistent challenges and hallucination, indicating unsolved status for ancient text recognition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Fragmentation in existing benchmarks across time, media, and scripts hampers fair evaluation and progress in ancient Chinese text recognition; a comprehensive, standardized dataset is needed to benchmark general and OCR-specialist models.

Method: Compile 2,700 images spanning multi-millennial, multi-medium, and multi-script dimensions; propose three medium-specific annotation standards (symbol, character, parsing standardization); evaluate both general Vision-Language Models (VLMs) and OCR-specialist models on Ancient-Bench.

Result: Ancient-Bench reveals that ancient Chinese artifact text recognition remains fundamentally unsolved, with persistent issues such as variant characters, specialized symbols, and model hallucination; dataset is publicly available at the provided GitHub link.

Conclusion: The benchmark addresses fragmentation by providing a standardized, multi-dimensional resource for fair evaluation, highlighting continued challenges and the need for further methodological advances in ancient-text recognition.

Abstract: Ancient Chinese artifact text recognition is fundamental to heritage digitization, and benchmarks for ancient texts are essential for evaluating current model capabilities. However, existing benchmarks suffer from ''fragmentation'', manifested in limited temporal coverage, limited medium diversity, and incomplete script types. Therefore, we present Ancient-Bench, a comprehensive benchmark of 2,700 images for ancient Chinese artifact text recognition, featuring three dimensions: Multi-millennial (spanning 3,000 years of character evolution), Multi-medium (covering nine artifact categories), and Multi-script (encompassing seven historical script forms). To enable consistent and fair evaluation across heterogeneous media, we further define three annotation standards tailored to the medium-specific characteristics of ancient texts: symbol standardization, character standardization, and parsing standardization. Extensive experiments on Ancient-Bench covering general Vision-Language Models (VLMs) and OCR-specialist models reveal that ancient Chinese artifact text recognition remains fundamentally unsolved, with persistent challenges in variant characters, specialized symbols, and hallucination. The dataset is available at https://github.com/SCUT-DLVCLab/Ancient_Bench.

</details>


### [75] [Temporal Sensitivity Analysis of Tessera Embeddings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27175)
*Julia Guerrero-Viu,Alex López-Cifuentes,Ignacio Pérez-Villar,Fabio Pacifici*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Embeddings from Tessera can replace or reduce temporal coverage for land-use/land-cover mapping with a tunable trade-off between window length and accuracy, varying by task and dataset.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: There is a need for precise, frequently updated LULC maps, but leading foundation models typically rely on a full year of observations; this study assesses how shorter temporal windows affect embedding utility.

Method: Frozen Tessera encoder; recompute embeddings over observation windows from 1 year down to 1 day; evaluate with a linear probe and a UNet segmentation head; compare to from-scratch models on LUCAS, DynamicEarthNet, and PASTIS-R.

Result: Embedding performance is task-dependent: on phenology-driven crops (PASTIS-R) embeddings achieve IoU 58.3, about 46% better than best from-scratch; on temporally stable classes (forests in DynamicEarthNet and LUCAS) gains require full supervision; embeddings are more label-efficient; reducing window from 1 year to 1 month costs 39% of PASTIS-R accuracy but only 5% for DynamicEarthNet; single-day embeddings enable land-cover classification in LUCAS at 3.4x chance level.

Conclusion: Temporal coverage is a tunable cost rather than a fixed prerequisite, enabling near-real-time mapping and faster LULC refresh cycles; embeddings yield strong benefits for phenology-driven tasks and label efficiency, with gradual, class-dependent degradation under shorter windows.

Abstract: Many Earth Observation applications need land-use/land-cover maps that are both precise and frequently updated, yet the strongest Earth Observation foundation models build their embeddings from a full year of observations. We present a controlled study of the temporal sensitivity of Tessera, one of these leading foundation models, for land-use/land-cover mapping. Keeping the encoder frozen, we recompute its embeddings over varying observation windows, from a full year down to a single day. We use them as inputs to a linear probe and a UNet segmentation head, benchmarking both of them against from-scratch networks on LUCAS, DynamicEarthNet, and PASTIS-R datasets. We show that the value of the embeddings is task-dependent. Where classes are separated by phenology, as for the crop types of PASTIS-R, they reach a mean Intersection-over-Union of $58.3$, about $46\%$ above the best from-scratch model. Where classes are temporally stable (e.g., forests in DynamicEarthNet and LUCAS), embedding-based and from-scratch models match only under full supervision. On both datasets, Tessera embeddings remain markedly more label-efficient. Degradation under shorter temporal windows is gradual and class-dependent. Contracting the window from one year to one month costs $39\%$ of the segmentation accuracy on PASTIS-R but only $5\%$ on DynamicEarthNet. Single-day embeddings still classify land cover in LUCAS at $3.4$ times the chance level. Our study shows that temporal coverage is therefore a tunable cost rather than a fixed prerequisite, opening regimes such as near-real-time mapping and faster land-use/land-cover refresh cycles.

</details>


### [76] [Parameter-Efficient pretrained-CT-to-MRI Transfer for Rectal Cancer Segmentation: Performance-Calibration Trade-offs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27178)
*Aneesh Rangnekar,Jorge Tapias Gomez,Joseph O Deasy,Harini Veeraraghavan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A Swin V2-based rectal cancer segmentation framework SWIFT and its parameter-efficient variants show efficient deployment with competitive accuracy, tumor detection, calibration; LoRA and decoder-ensemble variants achieve best calibration and parameter efficiency; results consistent with VoCo pretraining.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Need accurate rectal cancer segmentation in MRI for adaptive radiotherapy requiring high performance and calibrated uncertainty, with efficiency and robustness.

Method: Pretrain Swin V2 encoder on 10,444 public 3D CT volumes with DINOv2-style objective; adapt to T2-weighted MRI through four configurations: SWIFT (full fine-tuning), SWIFTe (decoder compression), SWIFTe-LoRA (low-rank adaptation), SWIFTe-LDE4 (LoRA-decoder ensemble). Evaluate on held-out 247-case single-institution MRI dataset from 1.5/3T GE scanners; metrics include geometric accuracy, tumor detection, radiomic agreement, and probability calibration; compare to baseline and temperature-scale calibrations.
